<claims>

1. The review mirror's identity digest covers the CONTENT of ignored
   paths, not merely their presence in the status listing. Evidence:
   `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:424-438` runs
   `git status --porcelain --ignored -uall -z`;
   `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:662-694` combines those status fields with
   a content manifest of the paths the listing names;
   `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:576-604` expands a directory subject
   recursively, so one `!! .claude/` entry pulls in every file beneath it.

2. That content coverage is deliberate and load-bearing, not an oversight
   that could simply be removed. Evidence:
   `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:673-679` states that editing an
   already-ignored file leaves the status listing byte-identical, so a
   status-only fingerprint verified clean across exactly the drift the
   check exists to catch.

3. The exposed window is the mirror build until the round wrapper exits,
   NOT the build until preparation. The same recorded
   `source_status_sha256` is compared against the live source three times:
   `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:457-480` at preparation,
   `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:273-288` inside the wrapper before the client
   runs, and `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:305-311` inside the wrapper after
   the client finishes. The third comparison spans the whole round.

4. Fusing the build and the preparation into one command is not
   constructible in this tool. The build runs the client context probe and
   writes the verified override file
   (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1718-1735`); the wrapper body embeds that
   file's SHA256 and refuses a mismatch
   (`skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md`, the `codex-fresh` wrapper block
   and the `verified-override-dispatch` contract region); and `-Prepare`
   installs that already-written body
   (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:264-267`). So the build must complete before
   the wrapper body can exist, and the body must exist before preparation
   can run.

5. The design's chosen remedy cannot weaken the gate. The plan inserts
   `Write-SourceDriftExplanation` between the refusal's `Write-Output` and
   its `exit 1` at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:845-851`
   (plan Task 2 Step 5), so it executes only after the comparison has
   already decided to refuse. The function returns no value a caller can
   branch on and prints only. Contest this if you can find any state of
   the sidecar file that changes an exit code.

6. The advisory sidecar's placement enters neither identity digest.
   `Get-StatusSha256` is called on the repository root and on the mirror
   directory only (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1324`, `:1408`, and the
   verify block at `:838-870`), and the plan writes the sidecar to
   `<MirrorPath>.source-manifest`, a SIBLING of the mirror and outside the
   repository (plan Task 1 Step 4).

7. Reading a file the build wrote does not violate this file's own stated
   rule against re-reading build-written values. That rule
   (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:47-52`) governs values that PIN
   something; the sidecar pins nothing and gates nothing.

8. The quiet-period rule is stated nowhere in `skills/`, so the plugin
   does not carry it to the repositories it is installed into. Evidence:
   `CLAUDE.md`'s own dispatch section states that no prose rule in
   `skills/` carries it, and its rule covers preparation to wrapper exit
   only, which claim 3 says is the wrong span.

9. Excluding the volatile directories from the digest was the obvious
   alternative and is rejected because `.claude/` and `.codex/` are
   instruction surfaces for the reviewer, so removing them from the tamper
   net defeats the check the digest exists to be. Evidence:
   `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md`'s `plugin-cache-reclassified` and
   `back-channel-auto-mirror` regions on why reviewer-reachable
   instruction text is treated as hostile.

10. The plan's contract-region mechanics are correct for this repository:
    a new region needs a whole-region pin in `evals/multi-model-verify/`
    plus a `DECLARED_REGIONS` entry
    (`evals/multi-model-verify/test_contract_coverage.py:624`,
    `:770-785`), and a pin asserted with `in text` against the raw file
    matches line breaks and indentation exactly
    (`evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py:842-870` for the
    existing form). Plan Task 3 Steps 1 to 3 do all three.

11. The plan is correct to leave `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` unbumped.
    Evidence: `CLAUDE.md`'s dev-loop section requires the bump AFTER the
    diff debate, because the debate is what moves the tree last.

12. The plan's test set is sufficient to prove the advisory property.
    `test_a_corrupted_source_manifest_still_refuses` and
    `test_a_clean_tree_verifies_with_no_source_manifest` (plan Task 2 Step
    1) cover both directions: a hostile sidecar cannot manufacture a pass,
    and a missing sidecar cannot manufacture a refusal.

# Design: the review mirror's identity window

**Filed 2026-09-05.** Reported from a session running the plugin against
KitnEssentials, which hit repeated `BLOCKED: the source status changed
since construction` refusals and could not tell why.

## The measured facts

1. `Get-StatusSha256` (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:662`) fingerprints a
   repo as `git status --porcelain --ignored -uall -z` PLUS the content
   manifest of every path that listing names.
   `Get-ContentManifest` (`:576`) expands a directory subject
   recursively, so a single `!! .claude/` status entry pulls the bytes of
   every file beneath it into the digest.
2. The content half is deliberate and the function says so: editing an
   already-ignored file leaves the status listing byte-identical, so a
   list-only digest would pass through exactly the tampering the check
   exists to catch.
3. The build-time `source_status_sha256` is re-checked at THREE points
   against the same recorded value:
   - `-Prepare` step 1a, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:457`
   - the wrapper, before the client runs, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:275`
   - the wrapper, after the client finishes,
     `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:310`, `throw 'the mirror changed while the
     round ran'`
4. Therefore the exposed window is **mirror build to wrapper exit**, not
   build to prepare. It spans the whole round.
5. The refusal names no path. `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:846` prints the
   class of change and stops.
6. Nothing in `skills/` states the constraint. This repo's `CLAUDE.md`
   carries a quiet-period rule covering `-Prepare` to wrapper exit only,
   and says in its own text that no prose rule in `skills/` carries it.
   The plugin therefore does not take the rule to the repos it is
   installed into.

## What the reporting session proposed, and why it is not the fix

The report proposed fusing the mirror build and `-Prepare` into one
command. That is refused on two grounds:

- It closes the first of three windows and leaves the largest one open. A
  round that takes ten minutes still voids if an ignored file moves while
  the reviewer reads.
- It is not constructible. The build runs the client context probe, the
  probe writes the verified override file, and the wrapper body embeds
  that file's SHA256 (`skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md`, the
  `verified-override-dispatch` region). The build must finish before the
  wrapper body can exist, and the wrapper body must exist before
  `-Prepare` can install it.

The real constraint is a QUIET PERIOD over the reviewed repository, from
the build until the wrapper exits.

## Decisions

**D1. The gate does not change.** No path is removed from the digest, no
carve-out is added, and no re-mint or reseal mode is introduced. A
volatile-path allowlist was considered and rejected for this cycle: the
directories that move on their own (`.claude/`, `.codex/`) are instruction
surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the tamper net defeats
the check. It is recorded as an open backlog item, not built.

**D2. The refusal explains itself, advisorily.** The build writes the
SOURCE side's content manifest to a SIBLING of the mirror directory,
`<MirrorPath>.source-manifest`. On the source-status refusal path only,
`-VerifyIdentity` reads that file and names the paths that appeared,
vanished, or changed content.

The advisory file is safe by construction and must stay that way:

- It is read ONLY after the comparison has already decided to block, so
  it can never turn a refusal into a pass. A missing, unreadable,
  truncated, or deliberately corrupted file changes the printed
  explanation and never the exit code.
- It is a sibling, not a child, of the mirror and lives outside the
  repository, so it enters neither `source_status_sha256` nor
  `mirror_state_sha256`.
- It pins nothing, which is why it may be a file at all. The header's
  rule that identity values are passed as arguments rather than re-read
  from a file the build wrote applies to values that PIN something; this
  one carries no authority.

**D3. The quiet period ships in the skill.** A contract region states it,
covering build through wrapper exit, so it travels with the plugin.

**D4. The build is ordered last.** `references/preflight-mirror.md` gains
a timing rule: every act that writes inside the reviewed repository
finishes before the mirror is built.

## Out of scope

- Any change to the digest's coverage (see D1).
- Item 91's cost question (the digest hashing a linked reference checkout
  repeatedly). Unrelated, already filed.
- Item 76's question about `.claude/skills` being materialised into the
  mirror. Unrelated, already filed.

## Success criteria

- An ignored-file edit between build and verify produces a refusal that
  NAMES the file, on both PowerShell hosts.
- Deleting or corrupting the advisory manifest changes no exit code in
  any case, proven by test.
- `test_contract_coverage.py` reports the new region locked.
- All six CI tiers and both PowerShell hosts pass.
# Mirror Identity Window Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make the review mirror's identity refusal name the paths that moved, and ship the quiet-period rule that the refusal enforces, so a session outside this repo can act on both.

**Architecture:** Three independent changes and no change to the gate. The build writes the source side's content manifest to a sibling of the mirror directory; the source-status refusal reads that sibling, on that path only, to name what appeared, vanished, or changed content; and the skill grows a contract region plus a timing rule stating that nothing may write inside the reviewed repository between the build and the wrapper's exit. The sidecar file is advisory: it is read after the comparison has already decided to block, so no state it can be in changes any exit code.

**Tech Stack:** Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1`), Python 3 with pytest (`evals/multi-model-verify/`), Markdown contract regions under `skills/`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md`

## Global Constraints

- `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` is **Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible and ASCII ONLY**. No smart quotes, no dashes outside the ASCII hyphen, no non-ASCII byte anywhere in the file.
- **No existing exit code may change in any existing case.** The gate stays fail-closed. Every new code path may only ADD printed output on a path that is already exiting 1.
- **Tests first.** Write the failing test, run it, watch it fail for the stated reason, then implement.
- **Do not bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` in this plan.** The bump happens after the diff debate, per `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Do not run pytest, the gates, or any repo-writing command while a review round is in flight.** That is the defect this plan documents.
- A new contract region needs BOTH a whole-region pin in `evals/multi-model-verify/` AND an entry in `DECLARED_REGIONS` in `evals/multi-model-verify/test_contract_coverage.py`.
- A pin asserted with `in text` against the raw file matches **line breaks and leading indentation exactly**. Write the Markdown first, then copy those lines byte for byte into the pin.
- Commits are lowercase imperative with no AI attribution. **A commit message must not contain a bare `-Word` token**: the family git guard reads it as a passed flag and denies the commit (backlog item 79).
- Work on a feature branch, never on `main`.

### Task 1: The build writes the source manifest beside the mirror

**Files:**
- Modify: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` (the `Get-StatusSha256` return at `:662-694`, two new functions beside it, and the record block at `:1737-1756`)
- Test: `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
- Produces:
  - `Get-StatusSha256($repo)` returns `@{ Ok = $true; Sha = <hex>; Manifest = <string[]> }` on success, unchanged `@{ Ok = $false; Reason = <string> }` on failure. `Manifest` holds `Get-ContentManifest`'s lines, each `"<relpath> <sha256hex>"`.
  - `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath($mirrorPath)` returns `"<mirrorPath>.source-manifest"`.
  - `Write-SourceManifestSidecar($path, $manifestLines)` returns `$true` or `$false`, never throws.
  - The build's record block gains one line, `source_manifest: <path>`, printed after `override:`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Add to `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py`, immediately after `test_the_record_carries_both_heads_and_the_source_status_hash`:

```python
def test_the_build_writes_the_source_manifest_beside_the_mirror(tmp_path):
    """The advisory sidecar the refusal reads to say what moved.

    A SIBLING of the mirror, never a child and never inside the repo:
    a child would enter mirror_state_sha256 and a file in the repo would
    enter source_status_sha256, and this one must enter neither.
    """
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    proc, _ = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    sidecar = tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest"
    assert sidecar.is_file(), proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    lines = sidecar.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
    assert any(line.startswith("ignored/secret.txt ") for line in lines), lines
    assert record_field(proc.stdout, "source_manifest") == str(sidecar)


def test_the_source_manifest_sidecar_enters_neither_identity(tmp_path):
    """The positive control for the sidecar's placement. If it landed
    inside the mirror or inside the repo, writing it would void the very
    identity it exists to explain, and this verify would refuse."""
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "identity: verified" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and confirm they fail for the stated reason**

Run: `python -m pytest evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py -k "source_manifest" -v`

Expected: `test_the_build_writes_the_source_manifest_beside_the_mirror` FAILS on the missing sidecar file. `test_the_source_manifest_sidecar_enters_neither_identity` PASSES already, because nothing writes a sidecar yet. That is correct: it is a regression guard for Step 3, not a red test.

- [ ] **Step 3: Return the manifest from `Get-StatusSha256`**

In `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1`, replace the final `return` of `Get-StatusSha256` (currently `:692-693`):

```powershell
    return @{ Ok = $true
              Manifest = @($content.Paths)
              Sha = (Get-CombinedSha256 $captured.Fields $content.Paths) }
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the two sidecar functions**

Insert immediately after `Get-StatusSha256`'s closing brace, before `$toplevel = $true`:

```powershell
function Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath($mirrorPath) {
    # A SIBLING of the mirror, never a child. A file inside the mirror
    # would enter mirror_state_sha256 and a file inside the repository
    # would enter source_status_sha256; this one must enter neither,
    # because it is written after both are measured and read only after
    # a comparison has already decided to refuse.
    return ($mirrorPath.TrimEnd("\", "/") + ".source-manifest")
}

function Write-SourceManifestSidecar($path, $manifestLines) {
    # ADVISORY ONLY. It pins nothing and gates nothing, so a failure to
    # write it is not a build failure - it costs a later refusal its
    # explanation and nothing else. That is also why it may be a file at
    # all: the header's rule about values passed as arguments rather than
    # re-read from a file governs values that PIN something, and this
    # value carries no authority.
    try {
        $utf8 = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
        [System.IO.File]::WriteAllLines($path, [string[]]@($manifestLines), $utf8)
        return $true
    } catch {
        return $false
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the sidecar and record its path**

In the record block, insert immediately BEFORE the existing `Write-Output ("mirror: " + $MirrorPath)` line (currently `:1737`):

```powershell
# The advisory source manifest, written before the record so the record
# can name it. Its failure is reported and never fatal.
$sidecarPath = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $MirrorPath
if (-not (Write-SourceManifestSidecar $sidecarPath $sourceStatus.Manifest)) {
    $sidecarPath = "unwritable"
}
```

Then insert one line immediately AFTER the existing `Write-Output ("override: " + $overrideFile)` line:

```powershell
Write-Output ("source_manifest: " + $sidecarPath)
```

Both insertions sit ABOVE the `-SkipProbe` block at the end of the file, so a `-SkipProbe` build writes the sidecar too. The tests depend on that.

- [ ] **Step 6: Run the tests and confirm they pass**

Run: `python -m pytest evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py -k "source_manifest" -v`

Expected: both PASS.

- [ ] **Step 7: Run the whole mirror module for regressions**

Run: `python -m pytest evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py -q`

Expected: all PASS. If the record-parsing tests fail, the new `source_manifest:` line was inserted inside a labelled block rather than after `override:`.

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**

```bash
git add tools/new-review-mirror.ps1 evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py
git commit -m "write the source content manifest beside the review mirror"
```

### Task 2: The source-status refusal names what moved

**Files:**
- Modify: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` (a new diff function and a new explanation function beside the sidecar functions, and the refusal at `:845-851`)
- Test: `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath($mirrorPath)` and `Get-StatusSha256`'s `Manifest` field from Task 1.
- Produces:
  - `Get-ManifestDrift($recordedLines, $liveLines)` returns `@{ Appeared = <string[]>; Vanished = <string[]>; Changed = <string[]> }`, each sorted by path.
  - `Write-SourceDriftExplanation($mirrorPath, $liveManifest)` writes lines to stdout and returns nothing. It never throws and never exits.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Replace the existing `test_source_drift_in_an_ignored_file_blocks_the_dispatch` body's final assertion block and add five tests, all directly after it:

```python
def test_the_refusal_names_the_ignored_file_that_changed(tmp_path):
    """The whole reason this task exists. The refusal used to name the
    CLASS of change and stop, so a session hitting it repeatedly could
    not tell a cache write from a planted file."""
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("edited after the copy\n")
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "source status" in proc.stdout.lower(), proc.stdout
    assert "changed" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
    assert "ignored/secret.txt" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout


def test_the_refusal_names_a_file_that_appeared(tmp_path):
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    (repo / "brand-new-input.txt").write_text("appeared after the copy\n")
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "appeared" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
    assert "brand-new-input.txt" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout


def test_the_refusal_names_a_file_that_vanished(tmp_path):
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").unlink()
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "vanished" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
    assert "ignored/secret.txt" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout


def test_a_missing_source_manifest_still_refuses(tmp_path):
    """The explanation is ADVISORY. Its absence costs the reason and
    never the refusal."""
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    (tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest").unlink()
    (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("edited after the copy\n")
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "source status" in proc.stdout.lower(), proc.stdout
    assert "unknown" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout


def test_a_corrupted_source_manifest_still_refuses(tmp_path):
    """The direction that matters. A sidecar an attacker can write must
    never turn a refusal into a pass, and it structurally cannot: it is
    read only after the comparison has already decided to block."""
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    (tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest").write_bytes(b"\x00garbage\xff\nno")
    (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("edited after the copy\n")
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "source status" in proc.stdout.lower(), proc.stdout


def test_a_clean_tree_verifies_with_no_source_manifest(tmp_path):
    """The other advisory direction. A tree that did not move verifies
    whether or not the sidecar survives, because the sidecar is not an
    input to the comparison."""
    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
    mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
    _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
    (tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest").unlink()
    proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
    assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
    assert "identity: verified" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the diff function**

Insert into `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` immediately after `Write-SourceManifestSidecar`:

```powershell
function Get-ManifestDrift($recordedLines, $liveLines) {
    # Both sides are "<relpath> <sha256hex>" lines, so the LAST space is
    # the separator: a pathname may hold spaces and a hex digest may not.
    #
    # A line this parser cannot read is DROPPED rather than raised. This
    # function runs only on a path that is already refusing, and a bad
    # explanation must never replace a correct refusal with an error.
    $recorded = @{}
    foreach ($line in @($recordedLines)) {
        $cut = ([string]$line).LastIndexOf(" ")
        if ($cut -lt 1) { continue }
        $recorded[([string]$line).Substring(0, $cut)] = ([string]$line).Substring($cut + 1)
    }
    $live = @{}
    foreach ($line in @($liveLines)) {
        $cut = ([string]$line).LastIndexOf(" ")
        if ($cut -lt 1) { continue }
        $live[([string]$line).Substring(0, $cut)] = ([string]$line).Substring($cut + 1)
    }
    $appeared = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
    $vanished = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
    $changed = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
    foreach ($p in @($live.Keys)) {
        if (-not $recorded.ContainsKey($p)) {
            [void]$appeared.Add($p)
        } elseif ($recorded[$p] -ne $live[$p]) {
            [void]$changed.Add($p)
        }
    }
    foreach ($p in @($recorded.Keys)) {
        if (-not $live.ContainsKey($p)) { [void]$vanished.Add($p) }
    }
    return @{ Appeared = @($appeared | Sort-Object)
              Vanished = @($vanished | Sort-Object)
              Changed  = @($changed  | Sort-Object) }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the explanation function**

Insert immediately after `Get-ManifestDrift`:

```powershell
function Write-SourceDriftExplanation($mirrorPath, $liveManifest) {
    # Runs ONLY after the source-status refusal below has been printed.
    # It prints what moved, or says plainly that it could not tell. It
    # never throws, never exits, and never returns a value a caller
    # could branch on, so no state of the sidecar can change a verdict.
    $sidecar = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $mirrorPath
    $recorded = $null
    try {
        if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $sidecar -PathType Leaf) {
            $recorded = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllLines($sidecar,
                (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)))
        }
    } catch {
        $recorded = $null
    }
    if ($null -eq $recorded) {
        Write-Output ("  what moved: unknown - no readable source manifest" +
            " at " + $sidecar)
        return
    }
    $drift = Get-ManifestDrift $recorded $liveManifest
    $groups = @(@("changed", $drift.Changed),
                @("appeared", $drift.Appeared),
                @("vanished", $drift.Vanished))
    $any = $false
    foreach ($g in $groups) {
        $names = @($g[1])
        if ($names.Count -eq 0) { continue }
        $any = $true
        Write-Output ("  " + $g[0] + " (" + $names.Count + "):")
        $shown = 0
        foreach ($n in $names) {
            if ($shown -ge 20) {
                Write-Output ("    ... and " + ($names.Count - 20) + " more")
                break
            }
            Write-Output ("    " + $n)
            $shown++
        }
    }
    if (-not $any) {
        Write-Output ("  what moved: no content difference, so the status" +
            " listing itself changed - a path took a different status" +
            " code, or a deletion-only entry moved. Those carry no bytes" +
            " and so appear in no manifest.")
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Call it from the refusal**

In the verify block, insert one line between the existing `Write-Output` of the source-status refusal and its `exit 1` (currently `:846-851`):

```powershell
        Write-SourceDriftExplanation $MirrorPath $liveStatus.Manifest
```

The refusal's own wording does not change, so the existing `"source status" in proc.stdout.lower()` assertions stay green.

Expected: all six PASS.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the region into SKILL.md**

Insert this block into `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md` immediately after the `<!-- contract:end -->` that closes the `back-channel-auto-mirror` region, at the same three-space indent as the text around it:

```markdown
   <!-- contract:start id=mirror-quiet-period -->
   NOTHING MAY WRITE INSIDE THE REVIEWED REPOSITORY from the moment the
   mirror is built until the wrapper exits. The identity digest covers the
   CONTENT of every path `git status --porcelain --ignored` names, ignored
   ones included, so a test-cache write, a plan-ledger append, a drift
   report or one new untracked file is enough. The same recorded digest is
   compared against the live source three times: at preparation, before the
   client runs, and after it finishes. The last of those spans the whole
   round, so this is a quiet period and not an ordering rule. A round that
   trips it is refused, its reply is NOT evidence, and the quota is spent
   for nothing. Queue every edit until the wrapper exits, however small and
   however unrelated it looks.
   <!-- contract:end -->
```

        # The reference stated no timing constraint at all, so a session
        # could build the mirror, run its gates, append its ledger, and

Expected: FAIL on the missing `BUILD THE MIRROR LAST`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the timing rule**

Append to `skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md`, as a new final section:

```markdown
## Timing

BUILD THE MIRROR LAST. Every act that writes inside the reviewed
repository finishes first: the gates, the plan ledger, the scratch notes,
the formatter. From the build until the round's wrapper exits, the
repository is quiet, and SKILL.md's `mirror-quiet-period` region states
why. The identity digest covers the content of ignored paths, so a
pytest cache directory or a ledger append is enough to refuse the
dispatch.

A build that has gone stale is not repaired and cannot be re-blessed:
there is deliberately no re-mint or reseal mode. Build again. It is
cheap next to a spent round, measured at about 92 seconds on a repo
carrying a linked reference checkout.

When a refusal names `the source status changed since construction`, the
lines beneath it name the paths that changed, appeared or vanished,
read from the `source_manifest` file the record block points at. That
explanation is advisory: it can be missing or wrong and the refusal
stands either way.
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run the test and confirm it passes**

Run: `python -m pytest evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py -k orders_the_build_last -v`

Expected: PASS.

- [ ] **Step 5: File backlog item 94**

Append to `BACKLOG.md`, after item 93, using the field order `Status`, `Cost`, `Pairs`, `Verified` that the linter requires for an OPEN item:

```markdown
## 94. The identity digest covers working state that moves on its own
Status: OPEN
Cost: every round is exposed for its whole duration to a write into an ignored working directory it does not control, and each such write costs one round of quota
Pairs: 76, 91
Verified: <run the digest command in Step 6 and paste the value here>

**Filed 2026-09-05 from a report by a session running the plugin against
another repository**, which hit repeated
`BLOCKED: the source status changed since construction` refusals and
could not tell why. Design:
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md`.

`Get-StatusSha256` fingerprints the content of every path
`git status --porcelain --ignored` names, and `Get-ContentManifest`
expands a directory subject recursively, so one `!! .claude/` entry
pulls in every file beneath it. The content half is deliberate and
load-bearing: editing an already-ignored file leaves the status listing
byte-identical, so a list-only digest would pass through exactly the
tampering the check exists to catch.

The exposure is the WHOLE ROUND, not the preparation gap. The recorded
digest is compared three times, and the third comparison runs after the
client finishes. In a repository whose ignored directories move on their
own, no ordering discipline closes that.

**What this cycle did and did not do.** The refusal now names the paths
that moved, the skill states the quiet period, and the reference orders
the build last. None of that narrows the digest, so the underlying
exposure is unchanged and this item stays OPEN.

**A candidate fix, rejected for now.** A declared allowlist of volatile
ignored paths held outside the content digest. Rejected because the
directories that move on their own, `.claude/` and `.codex/`, are
instruction surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the
tamper net defeats the check. Closing this item means a design that
narrows the digest without opening that hole, argued in the
`mirror-identity-gate` contract region and debated on its own.
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Compute the item's digest and fill it in**

Run: `python evals/tools/backlog_lint.py --digests BACKLOG.md`

Copy the digest printed for item 94 into its `Verified:` line, after the date `2026-09-05`.

## Self-review

**Spec coverage.** D1 (no gate change) is carried by the Global Constraints and by Task 2's two safety tests. D2 (advisory explanation) is Tasks 1 and 2. D3 (quiet period in the skill) is Task 3. D4 (build ordered last) is Task 4. The spec's success criteria map to Task 2 Step 6, Task 2 Step 1's two safety tests, Task 3 Step 4, and Task 5.

**Placeholders.** One value is deliberately left to be computed rather than guessed: item 94's `Verified:` digest, in Task 4 Step 6, because it is a hash of the item's own final text and cannot exist before that text does. The command that produces it is given.

**Type consistency.** `Get-StatusSha256` gains `Manifest` in Task 1 and Task 2 reads `$liveStatus.Manifest`. `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath` is defined in Task 1 and called in Task 2's `Write-SourceDriftExplanation`. The sidecar path `"<mirrorPath>.source-manifest"` is written in Task 1 Step 4 and asserted as `tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest"` in both tasks' tests.

## After the plan

This plan does NOT bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`. Per `CLAUDE.md`, the bump comes after the diff debate, because the debate is what moves the tree last. The sequence after Task 5 is: whole-branch review, mode-diff debate, version bump, merge, marketplace refresh, plugin update, and an install verified by content rather than by the cache directory's name.

 succeeded in 334ms:
    1: # new-review-mirror.ps1 - build the throwaway review mirror, perform
    2: # SKILL.md preflight-3 remediation inside it, and print the evidence the
    3: # debate record needs.
    4: #
    5: # The mirror is a FILE COPY that preserves .git, never a git clone: a clone
    6: # carries TRACKED FILES ONLY and the review inputs are routinely gitignored
    7: # (frozen plans, References/). Probed 2026-07-26 in KitnEssentials, where a
    8: # cloned workspace handed the reviewer a tree with nothing to review while
    9: # every route and containment check stayed green.
   10: #
   11: # Directory links are NOT copied through. robocopy runs with /XJD and the
   12: # tool re-creates each link as a junction after the last sweep, so the
   13: # reviewer reads the target through the same relative path while the
   14: # mirror carries none of its bytes. Measured 2026-09-05: one addon's
   15: # reference link held 14,884 files, doubling every mirror. The identity
   16: # digests still cover that content, because the manifest expands a
   17: # directory subject through a link on both hosts.
   18: #
   19: # This script never writes to the real tree, never dispatches a review, and
   20: # never decides to proceed. It stops immediately before the brief is
   21: # written, because the brief is the first artifact that is not evidence.
   22: #
   23: # Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible, ASCII ONLY.
   24: #
   25: # Exit codes: 0 built and clean, 1 blocked (reason on stdout), 2 script or
   26: # environment error.
   27: # TWO MODES. Build constructs the mirror and prints the record. Verify
   28: # re-checks that record against the live source and the live mirror
   29: # immediately before a dispatch, which is step 6 of the construction
   30: # bridge: the two-HEAD gate is worthless if it is only ever evaluated at
   31: # the moment the mirror is made.
   32: #
   33: #   Build:  -RepoRoot <src> -MirrorPath <dst> [-OverrideOut <p>] [-Force]
   34: #           [-SkipProbe] [-CodexCommand <exe>] [-ExtraInput <path>]...
   35: #   Verify: -VerifyIdentity -RepoRoot <src> -MirrorPath <dst>
   36: #           -ExpectedMirrorPath <path> -SourceHead <sha> -MirrorHead <sha>
   37: #           -SourceStatusSha256 <hex> -MirrorStateSha256 <hex>
   38: #
   39: # -ExtraInput names a file the mirror cannot inherit on its own - a
   40: # standards file living above the repo root, a spec kept outside the
   41: # tree - and may be repeated. Each one is copied into the mirror BEFORE
   42: # the baseline and the manifest are taken, so the identity record covers
   43: # it and the printed record enumerates it like any other mirror content.
   44: # There is deliberately no way to add one after construction, and no
   45: # re-mint or reseal mode: a tree that changed since it was measured is
   46: # exactly what -VerifyIdentity exists to refuse, so re-blessing one
   47: # would defeat it.
   48: #
   49: # The identity values are passed as ARGUMENTS rather than re-read from a
   50: # file the build wrote, for the reason the codex brief binding states
   51: # about its own expected hash: a file re-read later is mutable and would
   52: # silently redefine the value it is supposed to pin.
   53: param(
   54:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   55:     [switch]$VerifyIdentity,
   56:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   57:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$SourceHead,
   58:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   59:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$MirrorHead,
   60:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   61:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$SourceStatusSha256,
   62:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   63:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$MirrorStateSha256,
   64:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   65:     [string]$ExpectedMirrorPath,
   66: 
   67:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build", Mandatory = $true)]
   68:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   69:     [string]$RepoRoot,
   70:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build", Mandatory = $true)]
   71:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
   72:     [string]$MirrorPath,
   73: 
   74:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][string]$OverrideOut,
   75:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][switch]$Force,
   76:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][switch]$SkipProbe,
   77:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][string]$CodexCommand = "codex",
   78:     # Catches every remaining token so the BUILD can accept a repeated
   79:     # named flag. PowerShell's own parameter binder refuses to bind the
   80:     # SAME named parameter twice, even to an array - "Cannot bind
   81:     # parameter because parameter 'X' is specified more than once" -
   82:     # which is what a literal `-ExtraInput <path>` array parameter would
   83:     # do. -ExtraInput is parsed out of this list by hand below, and
   84:     # anything left over is a parameter this tool does not have.
   85:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build", ValueFromRemainingArguments = $true)]
   86:     [string[]]$BuildRemainingArgs
   87: )
   88: 
   89: # This script decodes git's pathnames as STRICT UTF-8 and refuses to guess
   90: # on a bad byte, then prints them. Without this line the print undoes that
   91: # care: [Console]::OutputEncoding defaults to the OEM code page - measured
   92: # IBM437 on both hosts here - so an accented pathname leaves as one 0x82
   93: # byte and a UTF-8 reader sees U+FFFD. The baseline and the manifest are
   94: # EVIDENCE, and evidence that renames a file is worse than no evidence.
   95: # The ambient encoding also varies by console, which is why this surfaced
   96: # as an order-dependent test rather than a constant one.
   97: [Console]::OutputEncoding = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
   98: 
   99: function Invoke-GitProcess($repo, $gitArgs) {
  100:     # Run git and hand back its stdout as RAW BYTES.
  101:     #
  102:     # Reading bytes rather than PowerShell's decoded string is the point.
  103:     # Windows PowerShell 5.1 decodes a native command's output with the
  104:     # console code page, and even a UTF-8 console override maps a malformed
  105:     # byte to U+FFFD SILENTLY. On this boundary a substituted character is
  106:     # a wrong pathname reported as a good one, so the decode happens once,
  107:     # downstream, and strictly.
  108:     #
  109:     # ARGUMENTS ARE WHITESPACE-FREE LITERALS AND THE REPO IS THE WORKING
  110:     # DIRECTORY. .NET Framework's ProcessStartInfo has no ArgumentList, so
  111:     # the command line is one string; passing the repo path through it
  112:     # would mean hand-quoting a value that can contain spaces, which is the
  113:     # defect class this change removes. The guard below refuses an argument
  114:     # this command line cannot express rather than trusting the caller.
  115:     #
  116:     # Every argument is QUOTED. Git for Windows runs on the MSYS2 runtime,
  117:     # which glob-expands an unquoted argument, and `*AGENTS.md` must reach
  118:     # git unexpanded or the back-channel enumeration silently narrows to
  119:     # whatever happens to sit in the working directory.
  120:     foreach ($a in @($gitArgs)) {
  121:         if ([string]$a -match '[\s"]') {
  122:             return @{ Ok = $false; Bytes = @()
  123:                       Reason = ("git argument '" + $a + "' carries" +
  124:                         " whitespace or a quote, which this command line" +
  125:                         " cannot express") }
  126:         }
  127:     }
  128:     $quoted = @($gitArgs | ForEach-Object { '"' + $_ + '"' })
  129:     $psi = New-Object System.Diagnostics.ProcessStartInfo
  130:     $psi.FileName = "git"
  131:     $psi.Arguments = ($quoted -join " ")
  132:     $psi.WorkingDirectory = $repo
  133:     $psi.UseShellExecute = $false
  134:     $psi.CreateNoWindow = $true
  135:     $psi.RedirectStandardOutput = $true
  136:     $psi.RedirectStandardError = $true
  137:     $mem = New-Object System.IO.MemoryStream
  138:     $p = $null
  139:     try {
  140:         $p = [System.Diagnostics.Process]::Start($psi)
  141:         # Drain stderr CONCURRENTLY. A full stderr pipe blocks git before
  142:         # it finishes writing stdout, and the stdout read below would then
  143:         # never return.
  144:         $errTask = $p.StandardError.ReadToEndAsync()
  145:         $p.StandardOutput.BaseStream.CopyTo($mem)
  146:         $p.WaitForExit()
  147:         [void]$errTask.Wait(5000)
  148:     } catch {
  149:         return @{ Ok = $false; Bytes = @()
  150:                   Reason = ("git could not be run: " +
  151:                             $_.Exception.Message) }
  152:     }
  153:     if ($p.ExitCode -ne 0) {
  154:         return @{ Ok = $false; Bytes = @()
  155:                   Reason = ("git exited " + $p.ExitCode) }
  156:     }
  157:     return @{ Ok = $true; Bytes = $mem.ToArray() }
  158: }
  159: 
  160: function ConvertFrom-NulCapture($bytes) {
  161:     # Turn a `-z` capture into its fields. Every failure here is a STOP.
  162:     #
  163:     # `-z` is why this exists. Git C-style-quotes a pathname whenever
  164:     # quoting would change it, and the trigger set is WIDER than the
  165:     # escapes alone: a plain SPACE is enough. Measured 2026-07-29, a
  166:     # directory named `M+ Timer` came back from `status --porcelain` as
  167:     # `?? "M+ Timer/"`, and `core.quotepath=false` did not suppress it.
  168:     # Under `-z` git emits the pathname verbatim and NUL-terminated and
  169:     # never quotes, so there is no escape grammar to decode.
  170:     $raw = [byte[]]@($bytes)
  171:     if ($raw.Length -eq 0) { return @{ Ok = $true; Fields = @() } }
  172:     if ($raw[$raw.Length - 1] -ne 0) {
  173:         return @{ Ok = $false; Fields = @()
  174:                   Reason = ("the -z capture does not end with a NUL, so it" +
  175:                     " was truncated and its last pathname is a fragment") }
  176:     }
  177:     # throwOnInvalidBytes, deliberately. The permissive decode substitutes
  178:     # U+FFFD and hands back a pathname that names a different file.
  179:     $utf8 = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)
  180:     $text = ""
  181:     try {
  182:         $text = $utf8.GetString($raw)
  183:     } catch {
  184:         return @{ Ok = $false; Fields = @()
  185:                   Reason = ("the -z capture is not valid UTF-8, so a" +
  186:                     " pathname in it cannot be read without guessing") }
  187:     }
  188:     # The capture ends with a NUL, so the split's LAST element is the empty
  189:     # string after it. Drop exactly that one. An empty field anywhere else
  190:     # is a real fault and belongs to the caller, not to this function.
  191:     $parts = $text.Split([char]0)
  192:     $fields = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
  193:     for ($i = 0; $i -lt $parts.Length - 1; $i++) {
  194:         [void]$fields.Add($parts[$i])
  195:     }
  196:     return @{ Ok = $true; Fields = @($fields) }
  197: }
  198: 
  199: function Test-SupportedPathname($value) {
  200:     # Under `-z` git never quotes, so whatever arrives IS the pathname.
  201:     # The question this answers is whether this tool can handle that
  202:     # pathname EXACTLY, in both senses:
  203:     #
  204:     #   1. Would resolving it risk naming the WRONG file? This is NOT a
  205:     #      Windows name validator: it does not check reserved device
  206:     #      names, trailing dots, length limits, or the other characters
  207:     #      Windows refuses. A syntactically fine name with no file
  208:     #      behind it is not this function's business; where it lands
  209:     #      depends on its status shape, and only ONE of those routes is
  210:     #      a stop. See the design record - the manifest stops on a
  211:     #      subject with no file, deletion-only entries are omitted
  212:     #      before that, and a back-channel match goes to remediation.
  213:     #   2. Can Format-StatusPathname record it in the porcelain line form
  214:     #      the baseline contract already uses?
  215:     #
  216:     # The admitted set is therefore an ordinary pathname, or one whose ONLY
  217:     # line-form quoting trigger is a SPACE - the one trigger the renderer
  218:     # reproduces. Everything else the line form quotes or escapes some
  219:     # other way is refused, and so is `>`.
  220:     #
  221:     # REFUSING rather than rendering is the deliberate choice for that
  222:     # residue. Git records it with git's own C-style encoder - NAMED
  223:     # escapes for tab, newline, `"` and `\`, and OCTAL for the rest,
  224:     # including the one reachable case, 0x7F as `\177`. Reproducing any
  225:     # of that would mean writing back the encoder this cycle deletes. A
  226:     # loud stop on a pathological name is the cheaper failure.
  227:     #
  228:     # The BACKSLASH refusal carries a second, heavier reason. git separates
  229:     # path components with a forward slash, so a backslash in a field is a
  230:     # literal character in a NAME - and `Join-Path` would then read it as a
  231:     # separator and resolve a DIFFERENT file, which the script would delete
  232:     # or hash under the name the baseline gave. That is false coverage
  233:     # rather than a refusal, which is the one outcome this preflight exists
  234:     # to prevent.
  235:     #
  236:     # `>` is refused for a reason of its own: Format-StatusRecord renders a
  237:     # rename as `<old> -> <new>`, and that arrow is only unambiguous while
  238:     # no pathname can contain `>`. The guard is what makes that true rather
  239:     # than assumed.
  240:     #
  241:     # 0x7F is refused, and it is the case that shows why this function is
  242:     # NOT named for Windows. Measured 2026-07-29: Windows CREATES
  243:     # `a<0x7F>b.txt`, and `git status --porcelain` prints it as
  244:     # `?? "a\177b.txt"` with AND without `core.quotepath=false`, while `-z`
  245:     # returns the byte raw. It is legal on disk and a quoting trigger this
  246:     # renderer cannot reproduce, so admitting it would put a bare name into
  247:     # a baseline the direct capture quotes.
  248:     #
  249:     # Non-ASCII is NOT refused. The old captures set `core.quotepath=false`
  250:     # and recorded an accented pathname raw, so it is not a trigger for the
  251:     # form this baseline uses.
  252:     $text = [string]$value
  253:     if ($text.Length -eq 0) { return $false }
  254:     foreach ($ch in $text.ToCharArray()) {
  255:         if ([int]$ch -lt 32) { return $false }
  256:         if ([int]$ch -eq 127) { return $false }
  257:         if ($ch -eq [char]34) { return $false }
  258:         if ($ch -eq '\') { return $false }
  259:         if ($ch -eq '>') { return $false }
  260:     }
  261:     return $true
  262: }
  263: 
  264: function ConvertTo-StatusRecord($fields) {
  265:     # Parse the `-z` status capture STRUCTURALLY. Returns @{Records=..} or
  266:     # @{Error=..}; a caller that cannot parse an entry must BLOCK, never
  267:     # skip, because a skipped entry is a silent hole in the manifest.
  268:     #
  269:     # THE RENAME FIELD ORDER IS INVERTED FROM THE LINE FORM. Measured
  270:     # 2026-07-29: the line form reads `R  <old> -> <new>`, and the same
  271:     # rename under `-z` reads `R  <new>` NUL `<old>` NUL - DESTINATION
  272:     # first, SOURCE second. Nothing about the record hints at the order,
  273:     # which is why it is stated here rather than left to a reader.
  274:     #
  275:     # Each field is the two status columns, one space, then the pathname.
  276:     $records = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
  277:     $all = @($fields)
  278:     $i = 0
  279:     while ($i -lt $all.Count) {
  280:         $field = [string]$all[$i]
  281:         $i++
  282:         if ($field.Length -lt 4) {
  283:             return @{ Error = ("status field '" + $field + "' is shorter" +
  284:                 " than two status columns, a space and a pathname") }
  285:         }
  286:         if ($field[2] -ne ' ') {
  287:             return @{ Error = ("status field '" + $field + "' has no space" +
  288:                 " where the status code ends, so this capture is not the" +
  289:                 " porcelain format this parser reads") }
  290:         }
  291:         $x = $field[0]
  292:         $y = $field[1]
  293:         $path = $field.Substring(3)
  294:         if (-not (Test-SupportedPathname $path)) {
  295:             return @{ Error = ("status entry '" + $field + "' names a" +
  296:                 " path this script cannot handle exactly, on one of the" +
  297:                 " guard's two grounds: resolving it would risk naming the" +
  298:                 " WRONG file, or it cannot be recorded unambiguously in" +
  299:                 " the baseline's line form") }
  300:         }
  301:         $source = $null
  302:         if ($x -eq "R" -or $x -eq "C" -or $y -eq "R" -or $y -eq "C") {
  303:             if ($i -ge $all.Count) {
  304:                 return @{ Error = ("rename or copy entry '" + $field +
  305:                     "' has no source field after it, so the capture is" +
  306:                     " truncated") }
  307:             }
  308:             $source = [string]$all[$i]
  309:             $i++
  310:             if (-not (Test-SupportedPathname $source)) {
  311:                 return @{ Error = ("rename or copy entry '" + $field +
  312:                     "' names a source this script cannot handle" +
  313:                     " exactly, on the same two grounds") }
  314:             }
  315:         }
  316:         [void]$records.Add(@{ X = $x; Y = $y; Path = $path
  317:                               Source = $source })
  318:     }
  319:     return @{ Records = @($records) }
  320: }
  321: 
  322: function Format-StatusPathname($path) {
  323:     # Reproduce the porcelain LINE form's quoting for one pathname, so a
  324:     # recorded baseline is byte-comparable with a capture taken by running
  325:     # THE STATUS COMMAND directly - which is what
  326:     # references/backup-lane.md requires of every round.
  327:     #
  328:     # The rule is one condition because the guard has already removed every
  329:     # other trigger. Measured 2026-07-29: `git status --porcelain` quotes
  330:     # each pathname independently and, once `"` , `\` and the control
  331:     # characters are refused, a SPACE is the only remaining trigger. In the
  332:     # same measurement a rename with a spaced source and a plain
  333:     # destination printed `R  "with space/a.lua" -> plain/a.lua`, and the
  334:     # reverse printed `R  plain/b.lua -> "with space/b.lua"` - each side on
  335:     # its own.
  336:     #
  337:     # Non-ASCII is deliberately NOT quoted. The old captures set
  338:     # `core.quotepath=false`, so an accented pathname was recorded raw, and
  339:     # this render must keep that shape rather than introduce quoting the
  340:     # baseline never had.
  341:     if ([string]$path -match ' ') { return ('"' + $path + '"') }
  342:     return [string]$path
  343: }
  344: 
  345: function Format-StatusRecord($record) {
  346:     # Render one record in git's own DISPLAY order, `R  <old> -> <new>`, so
  347:     # the recorded baseline keeps the shape references/backup-lane.md
  348:     # already describes and two captures stay comparable across this
  349:     # change.
  350:     #
  351:     # ONE WAY ONLY. Nothing re-parses this text: the manifest's subjects
  352:     # come from the RECORDS. That separation is the whole reason the arrow
  353:     # is safe to write here at all, and Test-SupportedPathname refusing `>`
  354:     # is what keeps it unambiguous.
  355:     $head = [string]$record.X + [string]$record.Y + " "
  356:     if ($record.Source) {
  357:         return ($head + (Format-StatusPathname $record.Source) + " -> " +
  358:                 (Format-StatusPathname $record.Path))
  359:     }
  360:     return ($head + (Format-StatusPathname $record.Path))
  361: }
  362: 
  363: function Invoke-GitFields($repo, $gitArgs) {
  364:     # Every git capture that produces PATHNAMES goes through here, so both
  365:     # of them answer the same questions the same way.
  366:     #
  367:     # `core.quotepath=false` is GONE from these captures and its absence is
  368:     # deliberate. The flag governed the DISPLAY form, and `-z` has no
  369:     # display form: measured 2026-07-29, `git ls-files -z` returned raw
  370:     # UTF-8 bytes with the flag ABSENT. Keeping a flag that does nothing
  371:     # invites a later reader to treat it as load-bearing.
  372:     #
  373:     # The console-encoding guard the old capture needed is gone for the
  374:     # same reason: nothing here goes through PowerShell's decoder. The
  375:     # bytes are read from the process stream and decoded once, strictly,
  376:     # in ConvertFrom-NulCapture.
  377:     $captured = Invoke-GitProcess $repo $gitArgs
  378:     if (-not $captured.Ok) {
  379:         return @{ Ok = $false; Fields = @(); Reason = $captured.Reason }
  380:     }
  381:     return (ConvertFrom-NulCapture $captured.Bytes)
  382: }
  383: 
  384: function Get-BackChannelEntry($repo) {
  385:     # One listing covering tracked, untracked AND ignored files. `--others`
  386:     # without `--exclude-standard` includes ignored paths. `*AGENTS.md`
  387:     # reaches any depth; `.agents/*` is anchored at the repo ROOT and does
  388:     # NOT, which is the asymmetry recorded in SKILL.md's
  389:     # enumeration-depth-asymmetry region. Do not restate "at any depth"
  390:     # here.
  391:     #
  392:     # Returns @{Ok=..; Entries=..; Reason=..}. A function returning a bare
  393:     # @() has its empty array unrolled by PowerShell, so the caller's
  394:     # variable becomes $null and a CLEAN repo reads exactly like a FAILED
  395:     # enumeration.
  396:     $r = Invoke-GitFields $repo @("ls-files", "--cached", "--others", "-z",
  397:                                   "*AGENTS.md", ".agents/*", '.kimi-code/*')
  398:     if (-not $r.Ok) {
  399:         return @{ Ok = $false; Entries = @(); Reason = $r.Reason }
  400:     }
  401:     # An entry this script cannot handle exactly is a stop, on either of
  402:     # the guard's two grounds - a name whose resolution would risk the
  403:     # WRONG file, or a name it could resolve but could not record.
  404:     # Reporting either as clean is the one outcome the whole preflight
  405:     # exists to prevent, and a back-channel is the case where that matters
  406:     # most.
  407:     foreach ($e in @($r.Fields)) {
  408:         if (-not (Test-SupportedPathname $e)) {
  409:             return @{ Ok = $false; Entries = @()
  410:                       Reason = ("the back-channel entry '" + $e + "' names" +
  411:                         " a path this script cannot handle exactly, so it" +
  412:                         " cannot be enumerated, deleted and recorded as a" +
  413:                         " single consistent fact") }
  414:         }
  415:     }
  416:     return @{ Ok = $true; Entries = @($r.Fields) }
  417: }
  418: 
  419: function Test-Tracked($repo, $path) {
  420:     & git -C $repo ls-files --error-unmatch -- $path 2>$null | Out-Null
  421:     return ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0)
  422: }
  423: 
  424: function Get-BaselineRaw($repo) {
  425:     # THE STATUS COMMAND, every capture without exception. Bare porcelain
  426:     # OMITS ignored paths and COLLAPSES an untracked directory to one
  427:     # entry; ignored content is the entire reason this workspace is a
  428:     # mirror.
  429:     #
  430:     # The BASELINE is this raw capture, status codes included
  431:     # (references/backup-lane.md). It is recorded verbatim and is NOT the
  432:     # same object as the manifest's subject list below.
  433:     #
  434:     # Same structured shape and same reason as Get-BackChannelEntry: an
  435:     # empty baseline is a legitimate state, and a bare @() return would be
  436:     # indistinguishable from a failed capture.
  437:     return (Invoke-GitFields $repo @("status", "--porcelain", "--ignored",
  438:                                      "-uall", "-z"))
  439: }
  440: 
  441: function Get-ManifestSubject($records) {
  442:     # Coverage is exactly the baseline's paths. Returns @{Paths=..} or
  443:     # @{Error=..}.
  444:     #
  445:     # Two dispositions are DEFINED and are not the same thing as skipping.
  446:     # A deletion-only entry is OMITTED, deliberately, because it has no
  447:     # bytes; an `RD` destination is a STOP. What must never happen is a
  448:     # third thing: an entry passed over because this function could not
  449:     # make sense of it. That is the silent hole in the manifest, and it is
  450:     # what the stops below exist to prevent.
  451:     #
  452:     # Takes RECORDS, never rendered text. A rename's destination is already
  453:     # the record's Path under `-z`, so there is no arrow to search for and
  454:     # no chance of splitting inside a pathname.
  455:     $paths = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
  456:     foreach ($r in @($records)) {
  457:         $x = $r.X
  458:         $y = $r.Y
  459:         # Deletion-only entries have no bytes to hash. HEAD plus the
  460:         # baseline already bind the absence, which is the whole content of
  461:         # the fact, so OMIT them.
  462:         if (($x -eq " " -and $y -eq "D") -or ($x -eq "D" -and $y -eq " ")) {
  463:             continue
  464:         }
  465:         # An `RD` destination no longer exists. That entry names no
  466:         # readable file, so it is a stop rather than a silent omission.
  467:         if ($y -eq "D" -and ($x -eq "R" -or $x -eq "C")) {
  468:             return @{ Error = ("baseline entry '" +
  469:                 (Format-StatusRecord $r) + "' names a destination that has" +
  470:                 " been deleted; the mirror is not in a state this manifest" +
  471:                 " rule can describe") }
  472:         }
  473:         [void]$paths.Add($r.Path)
  474:     }
  475:     return @{ Paths = @($paths) }
  476: }
  477: 
  478: function Get-FilesBeneath($start, $visited, $depth) {
  479:     # Every file beneath $start, INCLUDING files behind every directory
  480:     # link found under it, as full paths. Measured 2026-09-05 on both
  481:     # hosts: Get-ChildItem -Recurse enters a link only when the link IS
  482:     # the start path; from a parent it lists the link and does not pass
  483:     # through it. So each link beneath a subject gets a listing of its
  484:     # own, started at the link. $visited holds every resolved link
  485:     # target this expansion has entered, seeded by the caller with the
  486:     # repository root; a repeat is a refusal. Cross-vendor round 2
  487:     # (2026-09-05) showed the visited set alone is not a cycle guard: a
  488:     # RELATIVE symbolic-link target resolved against the link's spelled
  489:     # parent yields a new string on every level of a cycle reached
  490:     # through an outer junction. $depth is the bound that closes that:
  491:     # a link nested more than 16 links deep is refused. Returns
  492:     # @{Paths=..} or @{Error=..}.
  493:     if ($depth -gt 16) {
  494:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' sits more than 16 directory" +
  495:             " links deep; the manifest refuses a link graph that deep" +
  496:             " because a relative-link cycle presents exactly like it") }
  497:     }
  498:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
  499:     $startAttr = 0
  500:     try {
  501:         $startAttr = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($start)
  502:     } catch {
  503:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be read: " +
  504:                            $_.Exception.Message) }
  505:     }
  506:     if (($startAttr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0) {
  507:         $target = $null
  508:         try {
  509:             $target = (Get-Item -LiteralPath $start -Force -ErrorAction Stop).Target
  510:             if ($null -ne $target -and $target -isnot [string]) {
  511:                 foreach ($candidate in $target) { $target = $candidate; break }
  512:             }
  513:         } catch { $target = $null }
  514:         if ([string]::IsNullOrEmpty($target)) {
  515:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' is a directory link whose" +
  516:                                " target could not be read") }
  517:         }
  518:         $targetFull = ""
  519:         # WRAPPED, because this script never sets $ErrorActionPreference
  520:         # to Stop: a .NET exception here is NON-TERMINATING, so an
  521:         # unwrapped GetFullPath left $targetFull empty, put the empty
  522:         # string into the visited set, and returned Paths with no Error -
  523:         # a failed measurement reading as a clean one. Measured by the
  524:         # cross-vendor reviewer with a 270-character target
  525:         # (PathTooLongException), 2026-09-05.
  526:         try {
  527:             if ([System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted($target)) {
  528:                 $targetFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($target)
  529:             } else {
  530:                 $targetFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath(
  531:                     [System.IO.Path]::Combine(
  532:                         [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName(
  533:                             [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($start)), $target))
  534:             }
  535:         } catch {
  536:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' is a directory link whose" +
  537:                 " target " + $target + " could not be resolved to a full" +
  538:                 " path: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
  539:         }
  540:         if ($seamFailLinkTarget) {
  541:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' link target resolution" +
  542:                 " failed (test seam)") }
  543:         }
  544:         if (-not $visited.Add($targetFull.TrimEnd("\"))) {
  545:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' reaches " + $targetFull +
  546:                 " which this expansion already entered; the manifest refuses" +
  547:                 " a link graph that repeats or cycles") }
  548:         }
  549:     }
  550:     $found = $null
  551:     try {
  552:         $found = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $start -Recurse -File -Force `
  553:             -ErrorAction Stop
  554:     } catch {
  555:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be enumerated: " +
  556:                            $_.Exception.Message) }
  557:     }
  558:     foreach ($f in $found) { [void]$out.Add($f.FullName) }
  559:     $links = $null
  560:     try {
  561:         $links = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $start -Recurse -Directory -Force `
  562:             -ErrorAction Stop | Where-Object {
  563:                 ($_.Attributes -band [System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0 })
  564:     } catch {
  565:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be enumerated for" +
  566:                            " links: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
  567:     }
  568:     foreach ($l in $links) {
  569:         $inner = Get-FilesBeneath $l.FullName $visited ($depth + 1)
  570:         if ($inner.ContainsKey("Error")) { return $inner }
  571:         foreach ($p in @($inner.Paths)) { [void]$out.Add($p) }
  572:     }
  573:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
  574: }
  575: 
  576: function Get-ContentManifest($repo, $paths) {
  577:     # Directories expand RECURSIVELY to their files: a directory subject
  578:     # such as References/ is never one manifest entry, because a hash over
  579:     # a directory name identifies nothing.
  580:     # A directory link beneath a subject is expanded by a listing started
  581:     # at the link (Get-FilesBeneath), because a recursion from the parent
  582:     # does not pass through it on either host.
  583:     $files = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
  584:     foreach ($p in $paths) {
  585:         $full = Join-Path $repo $p
  586:         if (Test-Path $full -PathType Container) {
  587:             # -Stop, never the default: a swallowed enumeration error
  588:             # omits every file under an unreadable subdirectory and the
  589:             # manifest then reads as coverage of a tree it never saw.
  590:             # Seeded with the repository root: a link back onto the root
  591:             # is a cycle on its first step, not its second.
  592:             $visited = New-Object "System.Collections.Generic.HashSet[string]" (
  593:                 [System.StringComparer]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)
  594:             [void]$visited.Add([System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($repo).TrimEnd("\"))
  595:             $beneath = Get-FilesBeneath $full $visited 0
  596:             if ($beneath.ContainsKey("Error")) {
  597:                 return @{ Error = ("'" + $p + "': " + $beneath.Error) }
  598:             }
  599:             foreach ($fullName in @($beneath.Paths)) {
  600:                 $rel = $fullName.Substring($repo.Length + 1)
  601:                 [void]$files.Add($rel.Replace("\", "/"))
  602:             }
  603:         } elseif (Test-Path $full -PathType Leaf) {
  604:             [void]$files.Add($p.TrimEnd("/").Replace("\", "/"))
  605:         } else {
  606:             # A baseline path with nothing behind it is a stop. Skipping it
  607:             # would leave a hole in the manifest that reads as coverage.
  608:             return @{ Error = "baseline path '$p' has no file behind it" }
  609:         }
  610:     }
  611:     $unique = [string[]]@($files | Sort-Object -Unique)
  612:     [Array]::Sort($unique, [System.StringComparer]::Ordinal)
  613:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
  614:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
  615:     foreach ($rel in $unique) {
  616:         # A failed ReadAllBytes is NON-TERMINATING on both hosts
  617:         # (measured 2026-08-04), so without this catch $bytes keeps the
  618:         # PREVIOUS file's contents and this line records that hash for
  619:         # the file it could not read - deterministically, so the wrong
  620:         # value reproduces on every later run. Evidence that quietly
  621:         # names the wrong bytes is worse than no evidence.
  622:         $bytes = $null
  623:         try {
  624:             $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Join-Path $repo $rel))
  625:         } catch {
  626:             $sha.Dispose()
  627:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $rel + "' could not be read: " +
  628:                                $_.Exception.Message) }
  629:         }
  630:         $hex = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString($sha.ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
  631:         [void]$out.Add($rel + " " + $hex)
  632:     }
  633:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
  634: }
  635: 
  636: function Get-HeadSha($repo) {
  637:     # @{Ok=$true; Sha=..} or @{Ok=$false; Reason=..}. Structured for the
  638:     # same reason every other capture here is: a bare empty string cannot
  639:     # be told apart from a failed read, and an unread HEAD must never
  640:     # compare equal to anything.
  641:     $out = (& git -C $repo rev-parse HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
  642:     if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $out) {
  643:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = "could not resolve HEAD in $repo" }
  644:     }
  645:     return @{ Ok = $true; Sha = $out }
  646: }
  647: 
  648: function Get-CombinedSha256($fields, $manifestLines) {
  649:     # ONE digest over a raw status capture's fields plus a content
  650:     # manifest's lines, NUL-joined - the separator git already emits
  651:     # them with, so no field value can forge a boundary. Shared by the
  652:     # source-side fingerprint below and the mirror-side one at
  653:     # construction and verify time, so the two are computed the same way
  654:     # by construction rather than by two call sites staying in sync.
  655:     $joined = ((@($fields) + @($manifestLines)) -join "`0")
  656:     $bytes = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes($joined)
  657:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
  658:     try { $digest = $sha.ComputeHash($bytes) } finally { $sha.Dispose() }
  659:     return (($digest | ForEach-Object { $_.ToString("x2") }) -join "")
  660: }
  661: 
  662: function Get-StatusSha256($repo) {
  663:     # A non-HEAD fingerprint for whichever repo it is given: the SAME
  664:     # status capture the mirror already uses, PLUS the content manifest
  665:     # of the paths that status names, combined by Get-CombinedSha256.
  666:     # Used on the SOURCE for source_status_sha256 and on the MIRROR
  667:     # itself for mirror_state_sha256 - one algorithm, so the two
  668:     # fingerprints cannot drift apart by drifting the code that computes
  669:     # them.
  670:     #
  671:     # THE CONTENT HALF IS NOT OPTIONAL, and measured 2026-08-04 rather
  672:     # than assumed: editing an already-ignored file leaves the status
  673:     # listing byte-identical, so a status-only fingerprint verified clean
  674:     # across exactly the drift this check exists to catch. Status alone
  675:     # sees a path APPEAR or DISAPPEAR or change code; it does not see the
  676:     # bytes behind it change, and the bytes are the review input.
  677:     $captured = Get-BaselineRaw $repo
  678:     if (-not $captured.Ok) {
  679:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $captured.Reason }
  680:     }
  681:     $parsedStatus = ConvertTo-StatusRecord $captured.Fields
  682:     if ($parsedStatus.ContainsKey("Error")) {
  683:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $parsedStatus.Error }
  684:     }
  685:     $subj = Get-ManifestSubject $parsedStatus.Records
  686:     if ($subj.ContainsKey("Error")) {
  687:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $subj.Error }
  688:     }
  689:     $content = Get-ContentManifest $repo $subj.Paths
  690:     if ($content.ContainsKey("Error")) {
  691:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $content.Error }
  692:     }
  693:     return @{ Ok = $true
  694:               Sha = (Get-CombinedSha256 $captured.Fields $content.Paths) }
  695: }
  696: 
  697: $toplevel = $true
  698: 
  699: # THE THIRD TEST SEAM, declared here rather than beside the other two
  700: # because it is read inside Get-FilesBeneath, which runs during the
  701: # FIRST Get-StatusSha256 call - the source status taken before the copy,
  702: # and the verify mode's captures, both of which precede that block. Same
  703: # one-way rule: a BOOLEAN that only ADDS a failure inside the link
  704: # expansion, never supplies a value and never suppresses a measurement.
  705: $seamFailLinkTarget = [bool]$env:PARALLAX_MIRROR_SEAM_FAIL_LINK_TARGET
  706: 
  707: if (-not (Test-Path $RepoRoot)) {
  708:     Write-Output "ERROR: $RepoRoot does not exist"
  709:     exit 2
  710: }
  711: $RepoRoot = (Resolve-Path $RepoRoot).Path
  712: 
  713: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
  714: # VERIFY MODE - step 6 of the construction bridge, run immediately before
  715: # every fresh and resumed dispatch.
  716: #
  717: # WHAT IT PROVES: the two-HEAD gate proves committed-HEAD freshness.
  718: # Non-HEAD inputs are bound in the constructed mirror's manifest AT
  719: # CONSTRUCTION TIME, and changes after construction are caught by TWO
  720: # status-based fingerprints, one per side. The source-status comparison
  721: # catches source-side drift WHEN IT IS VISIBLE TO IT: changes that move
  722: # the status listing, or that alter the content of a path the listing
  723: # names. The mirror-state comparison catches the same class of change
  724: # made directly INSIDE the mirror after construction - an uncommitted
  725: # edit to a file the mirror's own HEAD still calls clean, or a file
  726: # added to the mirror - which the two-HEAD gate cannot see because
  727: # neither head moves. A tracked file git reports CLEAN, on either side,
  728: # is covered by neither fingerprint, so a raw-byte change surviving the
  729: # clean filter unchanged is NOT covered. The contract region carries the
  730: # same qualification; this comment said "are detected" flat until round
  731: # 3 of the mode-diff debate pointed out that the code and the contract
  732: # had drifted apart again, in the direction of the code claiming more.
  733: #
  734: # Two further refusals guard the comparison itself, both ahead of any
  735: # digest work: the source and the mirror must not be the same directory
  736: # - otherwise every remaining comparison is trivially satisfied whenever
  737: # the two heads already agree, which they do whenever the mirror needed
  738: # no remediation commit - and the live -MirrorPath must canonically
  739: # match -ExpectedMirrorPath. Both are CALLER ARGUMENTS in the SAME
  740: # invocation rather than values read back from the construction record,
  741: # so the guarantee is narrow: it catches a verify whose two path
  742: # arguments disagree, and it does NOT refuse a mirror rebuilt somewhere
  743: # else with both arguments updated to the new path.
  744: #
  745: # Neither status comparison is decoration. An edit to an untracked or
  746: # ignored review input moves NEITHER head, and that content class is
  747: # precisely what the mirror exists to carry, so a gate built only on
  748: # HEADs would be blind in the middle of the feature.
  749: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
  750: if ($VerifyIdentity) {
  751:     # Resolve THROUGH THE PROVIDER here too. Build does this below and
  752:     # verify did not, so a relative -MirrorPath resolved against the
  753:     # process location for Test-Path and against the provider location
  754:     # for git - the same divergence this file's own history block calls
  755:     # an ordinary PowerShell condition. Verify only reads, so the
  756:     # realistic failure was a spurious block rather than a wrong pass,
  757:     # but a tool should not teach one lesson in one mode and forget it
  758:     # in the other.
  759:     $MirrorPath = $ExecutionContext.SessionState.Path.GetUnresolvedProviderPathFromPSPath($MirrorPath)
  760:     $ExpectedMirrorPath = $ExecutionContext.SessionState.Path.GetUnresolvedProviderPathFromPSPath($ExpectedMirrorPath)
  761:     $shaRx = '^[0-9a-f]{40}$'
  762:     if ($SourceHead -cnotmatch $shaRx) {
  763:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the recorded source head is missing or not a" +
  764:             " commit id, so nothing was compared")
  765:         exit 1
  766:     }
  767:     if ($MirrorHead -cnotmatch $shaRx) {
  768:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the recorded mirror head is missing or not a" +
  769:             " commit id, so nothing was compared")
  770:         exit 1
  771:     }
  772:     if ($SourceStatusSha256 -cnotmatch '^[0-9a-f]{64}$') {
  773:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the recorded source status hash is missing or" +
  774:             " malformed, so nothing was compared")
  775:         exit 1
  776:     }
  777:     if ($MirrorStateSha256 -cnotmatch '^[0-9a-f]{64}$') {
  778:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the recorded mirror state hash is missing or" +
  779:             " malformed, so nothing was compared")
  780:         exit 1
  781:     }
  782: 
  783:     # BEFORE ANY DIGEST WORK. A source pointed at itself as the mirror
  784:     # would satisfy every remaining comparison trivially whenever the
  785:     # two heads already agree, which they do whenever the mirror needed
  786:     # no remediation commit.
  787:     $cmp = [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase
  788:     $rrNorm = $RepoRoot.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
  789:     $mpNorm = $MirrorPath.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
  790:     if ($mpNorm.Equals($rrNorm, $cmp)) {
  791:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the source and the mirror are the same" +
  792:             " directory ($MirrorPath), so nothing was compared")
  793:         exit 1
  794:     }
  795:     # The live -MirrorPath must canonically match -ExpectedMirrorPath.
  796:     # Both are caller arguments in this same invocation, so this catches
  797:     # a call whose two path arguments disagree. It does NOT refuse a
  798:     # mirror rebuilt elsewhere with both arguments updated.
  799:     $expNorm = $ExpectedMirrorPath.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
  800:     if (-not $mpNorm.Equals($expNorm, $cmp)) {
  801:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: -MirrorPath ($MirrorPath) does not match" +
  802:             " -ExpectedMirrorPath ($ExpectedMirrorPath) in this same call")
  803:         exit 1
  804:     }
  805: 
  806:     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $MirrorPath -PathType Container)) {
  807:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the mirror at $MirrorPath is gone, so its" +
  808:             " identity could not be measured")
  809:         exit 1
  810:     }
  811: 
  812:     $liveSource = Get-HeadSha $RepoRoot
  813:     if (-not $liveSource.Ok) {
  814:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: " + $liveSource.Reason + " - an unread source" +
  815:             " head is never a matching one")
  816:         exit 1
  817:     }
  818:     if ($liveSource.Sha -ne $SourceHead) {
  819:         Write-Output ("BLOCKED: the source head moved since construction" +
  820:             " (recorded " + $SourceHead + ", live " + $liveSource.Sha +
  821:             ") - rebuild the mirror")
  822:         exit 1
  823:     }
  824: 
  825:     $liveMirror = Get-HeadSha $MirrorPath
  826:     if (-not $liveMirror.Ok) {
