1: ---
2: name: multi-model-verify
3: description: Use when planning a reference port, an API-sensitive module, or any change risky enough to need cross-model verification before implementation, or when an implemented diff must be checked against its plan and reference before merge. Fires automatically alongside superpowers requesting-code-review via the review-companion hook.
4: ---
5: 
6: # Multi-Model Verify
7: 
8: ## Overview
9: 
10: Two equal-weight advisors — this session and a cross-vendor reviewer driven
11: through the codex CLI (canonical reviewer model:
12: references/model-prompting-notes.md) — verify and refute each other's
13: claims before the cheap implementer touches code. The reviewer lane's
14: documented fabrication risk (METR; see model-prompting-notes.md) is
15: mitigated by the debate structure — evidence grounding plus mutual
16: refutation — not by down-weighting either side. The PRIMARY reviewer
17: lane (codex) is the default; a second cross-vendor BACKUP reviewer lane
18: (references/backup-lane.md — REQUIRED READING before any backup round)
19: substitutes ONLY through the fallbacks.md consent gate — auto-qualified
20: by the classes named there, manual on user request — with the same
21: protocol, a different transport, and `Verification status: FULL`
22: preserved.
23: Mode diff's debate is preceded by a REQUIRED whole-branch review from
24: the fable-reviewer seat (agents/fable-reviewer.md), and the user may
25: convene multi-reviewer PANELS (references/panels.md) in either mode.
26: 
27: **REQUIRED READING before the first round:** references/debate-protocol.md.
28: 
29: Companion to superpowers, not a replacement: it fills the cross-model review
30: gap superpowers rules out of scope. `/codex:adversarial-review` remains the
31: human-invoked pre-merge gate; if the codex plugin's stop-review-gate is
32: toggled on, its stop-time review overlaps mode `diff` — expected, not a bug.
33: 
34: ## When to use
35: 
36: - **Mode `plan`** — after superpowers brainstorming proposes approaches for a
37:   port or API-sensitive module, before the implementation plan is written.
38: - **Mode `diff`** — after implementation, alongside superpowers
39:   requesting-code-review (the review-companion hook injects this
40:   automatically with the matching base/head SHAs).
41: - NOT for plain bugfixes, docs or release chores, GUI tweaks, or lint sweeps.
42: 
43: ## Preflight (both modes)
44: 
45: 1. `codex --version` must succeed, and `codex login status` must report
46:    `Logged in using ChatGPT` — exit 0 alone also passes an API-key login,
47:    which rides different billing. On failure follow references/fallbacks.md
48:    (the consent gate may offer the cross-vendor backup lane —
49:    references/backup-lane.md — before any single-vendor degraded mode;
50:    never silently skip cross-vendor review).
51: 2. For port work: the reference source must exist under `References/`
52:    (quote paths — some contain spaces, e.g. `References/M+ Timer`). Missing
53:    → **HARD STOP before any debate round runs**: ask the user for the path
54:    and go no further — do not run a codex exchange about the absence, do
55:    not debate from memory, do not start a degraded mode. If the folder
56:    holds version subdirectories (e.g. a `v1.4/` next to a `v1.1_old/`), use
57:    the newest unless the user names one, and cite the versioned path:
58:    `References/<name>/<version>/<file>:<line>`.
59:    For non-port work there is no reference folder — claims ground in the
60:    project's own source, specs, or upstream docs instead, same strike rule.
61: 3. The reviewed repo must carry no AGENTS.md, no `.agents/` entries, and no
62:    `.kimi-code/` entries: codex auto-ingests AGENTS.md as instructions, and
63:    it advertises repo-level `.agents/skills/*/SKILL.md` to the model, which
64:    read a planted one as its FIRST action (both probed 2026-07-24: the
65:    planted AGENTS.md controlled the reviewer's reply; the planted skill
66:    entered its context; see model-prompting-notes.md) — back-channels into
67:    the auditor that break independence. kimi-code, the backup lane's
68:    client, documents `.kimi-code/skills/` as a project-level discovery
69:    root too (references/backup-lane.md), so the sweep covers it
70:    identically. That 2026-07-31 comparison was CONFOUNDED: `Skill` was
71:    denied in both arms, so the comparison did not isolate the flag.
72:    Re-probed 2026-08-03, the flag DOES suppress the home root while its
73:    target stays empty (references/backup-lane.md). This enumeration is
74:    the PRIMARY control for the reviewed tree's skills and agents, not
75:    defence in depth. Enumerate the whole
76:    tree in one listing — `git ls-files --cached --others '*AGENTS.md'
77:    '.agents/*' '.kimi-code/*'` — which covers tracked, untracked, AND
78:    ignored files: `--others` without `--exclude-standard` lists ignored
79:    paths, re-verified 2026-07-28, and `.git` itself is never listed.
80:    <!-- contract:start id=enumeration-depth-asymmetry -->
81:    The two pathspecs do not reach equally far. `*AGENTS.md` carries a
82:    leading star, so it lists a nested AGENTS.md at any depth. `.agents/*`
83:    is anchored at the repo ROOT, so a nested `sub/.agents/skills/x/` is
84:    NOT listed. Measured 2026-07-28 on codex-cli 0.144.1: the harness
85:    advertises a ROOT `.agents/skills` entry and does not advertise a
86:    nested one, so the asymmetry is not reachable today, and the client
87:    probe below reads what was loaded rather than where it might live.
88:    Widen the pathspec if that ever changes.
89:    <!-- contract:end -->
90:    <!-- contract:start id=back-channel-auto-mirror -->
91:    If present: BUILD THE MIRROR AND REPORT. Do NOT ask first - every
92:    deletion happens in a file COPY, and the remediation commit runs with
93:    repository hooks suppressed, so nothing in the reviewed tree executes
94:    and there is no destructive act to consent to. What was found is
95:    still EVIDENCE and still goes in the debate record with its paths,
96:    and the post-mirror re-enumeration must still come back empty before
97:    any round dispatches. A mirror that cannot be built - path budget
98:    blown, scratch unavailable, hooks not suppressible - is BLOCKED,
99:    never a fallback to dispatching over the real tree.
100:    <!-- contract:end -->
101:    Run
102:    `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1 -RepoRoot <repo> -MirrorPath <scratch>`
103:    at a SHORT `<scratch>` directly under the temp directory, never inside
104:    the session scratchpad, to build the **review mirror**, remediate the
105:    offending entries there, re-run the enumeration and the client probe
106:    below against it, and print the record block; empty enumeration
107:    output is the evidence. Whether the removal needs a commit branches on
108:    tracked-ness; references/backup-lane.md states that branch and the
109:    hook behaviour that comes with it. Full construction detail and the
110:    mirror's identity fields are in references/preflight-mirror.md.
111: 
112:    **The reviewer's own machine is the second half of this check, and the
113:    enumeration above cannot see it.** Run
114:    `tools/codex-context-probe.ps1 -WorkDir <dispatch cwd> -SuppressSkills -OverrideOut <verified-override-file> -Json`
115:    before round 1, with a FRESH scratch path for the override file (the
116:    mirror script runs it for you and prints the same result). It renders
117:    the model-visible prompt with `codex debug prompt-input`, which spends
118:    no tokens, sorts every ADVERTISED SKILL by the directory it came from,
119:    and checks the named instruction and feature blocks around them:
120:    anything inside the reviewed tree STOPS and is remediated in the
121:    mirror, anything from the codex plugin cache must be empty, and the
122:    global `AGENTS.md` plus any surviving home-scoped skill is recorded in
123:    the debate record with its path.
124:    <!-- contract:start id=client-context-probe -->
125:    A probe that cannot be taken, that exits non-zero, that returns output
126:    this parser cannot read, or that finds a named block missing is a
127:    transport failure and stops the round. It is never read as a clean
128:    result: an unmade measurement and a clean one must never look alike.
129:    <!-- contract:end -->
130: 
131:    <!-- contract:start id=plugin-cache-reclassified -->
132:    The user's codex plugin cache is NOT a harmless environment note.
133:    Measured 2026-07-28 on codex-cli 0.144.1, it delivered 31 skills into
134:    the reviewer's context, one of whose descriptions alone instructs the
135:    model to invoke a skill before answering anything; a reviewer in
136:    another session adopted it and answered without opening the plan.
137:    `--disable plugins --disable apps` removes it, and the probe's second
138:    pass is what proves the removal happened.
139:    <!-- contract:end -->
140: 
141:    <!-- contract:start id=client-probe-scope-limit -->
142:    State what a clean probe means, and never more. It means exactly this:
143:    no skill is advertised, no plugin or apps block is present, and no
144:    instruction source sits inside the reviewed tree. Three things it does
145:    NOT mean. The global `AGENTS.md` above survives a clean probe and is
146:    still instructing the reviewer; the probe records it rather than
147:    removing it. It says nothing about the TOOL surface, which is not in
148:    the prompt and is measured separately by the tool-surface probe in
149:    references/model-prompting-notes.md, where a clean result is a
150:    mitigation, never proof of removal. And full flag parity with the
151:    dispatch cannot be REQUESTED: `prompt-input` rejects `--sandbox` and
152:    `-m`, so whether either changes rendered content is UNVERIFIED. Do not
153:    call a passing probe full reviewer isolation.
154:    <!-- contract:end -->
155: 
156: ## Mode plan
157: 
158: 1. Draft the session position: chosen approach, port-fidelity claims, and the
159:    API/behavior risk register. Every reference claim cites a
160:    `References/<name>/<file>:<line>` actually read this session — anchor
161:    EVERY file with its full path the first time you cite it, manifests and
162:    secondary files included (shorthand only once that file is anchored);
163:    every API claim cites authoritative local docs (WoW projects:
164:    `.wow-api-reference/` or a dated in-game probe result) — never memory.
165:    The position has no separate artifact — it IS the claims section of the
166:    step-2 brief.
167: <!-- call:codex-fresh -->
168: 2. Compose the reviewer's debate brief per references/model-prompting-notes.md, write
169:    it to a scratchpad file, then write this wrapper body to `<wrapper-file>` — as
170:    a FILE, never from a here-string, whose terminator cannot survive this block's
171:    indentation — and prepare it with the tool. Never run it inline: a foreground
172:    call owns the session, so nobody can see the round or talk to the agent while
173:    it runs. The ceiling is not a kill; visibility is the reason.
174: 
175:    The wrapper body is today's block with the exit scaffolding added:
176: 
177:    <!-- wrapper:codex-fresh -->
178:    ```powershell
179:    $code = 1
180:    $priorOutputEncoding = $OutputEncoding
181:    try {
182:    $OutputEncoding = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
183:    $brief = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText("<brief-file>", (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)))
184:    $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("<verified-override-file>")
185:    $seen = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString(([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()).ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
186:    if ($seen -cne "<override-sha256>") { throw "the override file changed after the probe verified it" }
187:    $override = (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)).GetString($bytes)
188:    $brief | codex exec --sandbox read-only --disable plugins --disable apps --disable memories -c mcp_servers.node_repl.enabled=false -c $override -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message $PSScriptRoot/reply - > $PSScriptRoot/transcript 2>&1
189:    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
190:    } catch { $code = 1 } finally { $OutputEncoding = $priorOutputEncoding }
191:    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText("$PSScriptRoot/exit", "$code")
192:    exit $code
193:    ```
194: 
195:    `$PSScriptRoot` is the dispatch directory, because the tool installs the
196:    body into it as `body.ps1` and runs it there as a child process. That
197:    removes the need to pass a path in and removes one more thing a copy
198:    can get wrong.
199: 
200:    Run `-Prepare`, naming `-DispatchHost` explicitly as the caller's own
201:    host — the bare token `pwsh` or `powershell`, DERIVED from
202:    `(Get-Process -Id $PID).Path` rather than guessed; the tool refuses a
203:    full path — and passing `-WorkdirEvidence` with the resolved
204:    mirror path — the codex lane's transcript header carries a `workdir:`
205:    line naming it, confirmed on a real round:
206: 
207:    ```powershell
208:    & (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path -NoProfile -File ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/tools/dispatch-round.ps1 -Prepare -DispatchDir <dispatch-dir> -WrapperBody <wrapper-file> -ReceiptPath <receipt-file> -Round <label> -WorkingDirectory <mirror-path> -RepoRoot <repo-root> -SourceHead <source-head> -MirrorHead <mirror-head> -SourceStatusSha256 <source-status-sha256> -MirrorStateSha256 <mirror-state-sha256> -ExpectedMirrorPath <mirror-path> -DispatchHost <dispatch-host> -PriorStateFile <prior-state-file> -WorkdirEvidence <mirror-path> -Json
209:    ```
210: 
211:    `<receipt-file>` is a FRESH path, alongside the fresh reply and
212:    transcript paths already required; `-Prepare` refuses one that
213:    exists. `<label>` names the lane and the round, as in `Astra R1`.
214:    `-Prepare` prints `command` and `taskName`: dispatch it as a harness background command, running `command` exactly as printed, under the `taskName` the tool printed, and STOP — but never END THE TURN with the round unfinished (references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-operation).
215: 
216:    On the completion notification for that exact task, read the harness output file: the exit code of that exact task is the result, never re-read the dispatch directory for a verdict — **0 means `reply-present` and nothing else; 2 is a parameter-binding failure or an internal execution error; 1 is every other state, named on the wrapper's last stdout line.** The states -Classify computes are references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-states.
217: 
218:    Both encoding lines are load-bearing on Windows PowerShell 5.1
219:    (references/model-prompting-notes.md).
220: 
221:    <!-- contract:start id=verified-override-dispatch -->
222:    The `-c` value MUST be the file the probe wrote with `-OverrideOut`, on
223:    round 1 and on every resume, read as raw bytes whose hash is checked
224:    against the probe's report before use. The two feature flags alone
225:    still leave the user's own skills directory and codex's built-in skills
226:    advertised, which was 29 of the original 60 when this was measured;
227:    only the generated override removes those, and only the probe's second
228:    pass proves it did. A dispatch that omits the override, or carries a
229:    value the probe did not verify, is a transport failure, because the
230:    measurement then describes a configuration the reviewer never received.
231:    <!-- contract:end -->
232: 
233:    `<canonical-model-id>` and `<canonical-effort>` are the two declarations
234:    in references/model-prompting-notes.md — read the literal values from
235:    there, never from memory (the reviewer swaps by editing that one file,
236:    and a remembered id silently defeats the swap). Apply that file's env
237:    hygiene to the invocation.
238: 
239:    Backup lane: same protocol, transport and per-round evidence per references/backup-lane.md.
240: 
241:    Panels: any reviewer-lane combination per references/panels.md.
242: 
243:    From `<transcript-file>`: verify the effective route — the header's
244:    `model:`, `provider:`, and `reasoning effort:` lines against the
245:    canonical declarations, and the `sandbox:` line reads `read-only`, per
246:    model-prompting-notes.md; a mismatch is a transport failure
247:    (fallbacks.md), never a review result — and capture the `session id:`
248:    line. Read the reviewer's reply from `<reply-file>` — the transcript
249:    logs every file the reviewer reads and can run tens of KB, with the
250:    reply buried at the bottom (a reused path, which `-Prepare` refuses,
251:    would read exactly like success).
252:    Per-round evidence: bind the reply to the brief THIS side sent with
253:    `tools/read-codex-round-evidence.ps1` — `-Fresh` at round 1, `-Resume`
254:    after. `-PriorState` is captured immediately before EVERY dispatch —
255:    an inventory of the session root before round 1, then each round's
256:    own state — and is never inherited from the last clean round; a state
257:    with a missing field is refused, never assumed empty. Pass
258:    `-SealedPriorStateSha256` with the receipt's `priorStateSha256` on
259:    EVERY call — mandatory here though optional on the binder for other
260:    callers; `sealed: "not-checked"` is a transport failure, not a clean
261:    round. A verdict other
262:    than clean is class `brief-attribution` (fallbacks.md): the reply is
263:    discarded unread. A clean verdict is client-echo evidence — what the
264:    client recorded, never what any server received.
265: 
266: <!-- call:codex-resume -->
267: 3. Later rounds keep the reviewer's state by resuming that session — flags
268:    MUST precede the resume subcommand (flags after it are a usage error).
269:    Compose the rebuttal and launch the same wrapper shape as round 1,
270:    with the resumed call:
271: 
272:    <!-- wrapper:codex-resume -->
273:    ```powershell
274:    $code = 1
275:    $priorOutputEncoding = $OutputEncoding
276:    try {
277:    $OutputEncoding = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
278:    $brief = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText("<brief-file>", (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)))
279:    $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("<verified-override-file>")
280:    $seen = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString(([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()).ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
281:    if ($seen -cne "<override-sha256>") { throw "the override file changed after the probe verified it" }
282:    $override = (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)).GetString($bytes)
283:    $brief | codex exec --sandbox read-only --disable plugins --disable apps --disable memories -c mcp_servers.node_repl.enabled=false -c $override -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message $PSScriptRoot/reply resume <SESSION_ID> - > $PSScriptRoot/transcript 2>&1
284:    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
285:    } catch { $code = 1 } finally { $OutputEncoding = $priorOutputEncoding }
286:    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText("$PSScriptRoot/exit", "$code")
287:    exit $code
288:    ```
289: 
290:    Run `-Prepare` the same way as round 1, with `-DispatchHost` and
291:    `-WorkdirEvidence` set the same way. A resumed round needs the
292:    mirror at the path its identity was recorded at: if the tree had to be
293:    rebuilt mid-debate, rebuild it at the SAME path
294:    with `-Force` and re-record its identity fields, because a mirror at a new
295:    path makes the binder refuse the resumed slice on `cwd` and the round is
296:    lost. If it cannot be rebuilt there, dispatch FRESH rather than resume.
297: 
298:    ```powershell
299:    & (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path -NoProfile -File ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/tools/dispatch-round.ps1 -Prepare -DispatchDir <dispatch-dir> -WrapperBody <wrapper-file> -ReceiptPath <receipt-file> -Round <label> -WorkingDirectory <mirror-path> -RepoRoot <repo-root> -SourceHead <source-head> -MirrorHead <mirror-head> -SourceStatusSha256 <source-status-sha256> -MirrorStateSha256 <mirror-state-sha256> -ExpectedMirrorPath <mirror-path> -DispatchHost <dispatch-host> -PriorStateFile <prior-state-file> -WorkdirEvidence <mirror-path> -Json
300:    ```
301: 
302:    `-Prepare` prints `command` and `taskName`: dispatch it as a harness background command, running `command` exactly as printed, under the `taskName` the tool printed, and STOP — but never END THE TURN with the round unfinished (references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-operation).
303: 
304:    On the completion notification for that exact task, read the harness output file: the exit code of that exact task is the result, never re-read the dispatch directory for a verdict — **0 means `reply-present` and nothing else; 2 is a parameter-binding failure or an internal execution error; 1 is every other state, named on the wrapper's last stdout line.** The states -Classify computes are references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-states.
305: 
306:    Bind the reply the same way as round 1, with `-Resume` in place of
307:    `-Fresh`, and pass `-SealedPriorStateSha256` with THIS round's
308:    `priorStateSha256`, captured immediately before this dispatch.
309: 
310:    The preamble repeats in full every round. Rounds are separate shell
311:    invocations, so a `$override` set in round 1 does not exist in round 3,
312:    and one verification does not cover a file that can change between
313:    rounds.
314: 
315:    Each resume's header must echo the resumed `session id:` and the same
316:    effective route — the round-1 check repeated, `sandbox:` included.
317:    Sandbox mode has NO continuity across resumes, so the flag is re-pinned
318:    on every one; references/model-prompting-notes.md carries the probe that
319:    measured it and what a dropped flag does.
320: 
321: 4. Iterate per debate-protocol.md until convergence or the round cap, then
322:    escalate any unresolved points to the user with both positions stated.
323: 5. Freeze the converged plan per references/frozen-plan-format.md under the
324:    project's superpowers plans dir (KitnEssentials:
325:    `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`; other projects: the superpowers default
326:    `docs/superpowers/plans/`).
327: 
328: ## Mode diff
329: 
330: Same transport and protocol. First read the frozen plan's debate record and
331: its **Verification status** field.
332: 
333: Required before round 1: the agents/fable-reviewer.md whole-branch review runs on the same range, its raw reply is retained as a range-bound artifact, and the round-1 brief cites that artifact with the session's per-finding adjudications.
334: The session adjudicates each review finding with evidence — accept,
335: refute, or ESCALATE into the debate — before any reviewer lane sees
336: them; the review is the debate's required input, never its verdict,
337: and the debate record names the artifact path.
338: 
339: Then the brief carries the frozen plan
340: path, the base/head SHAs superpowers code review used, and the
341: `git diff base..head` output. Both sides check **spec fidelity** (drift from
342: the frozen plan — the implementer makes zero judgment calls, so any drift is
343: a finding, with one carve-out: envelope-designated escalation-lane DECISIONS
344: are adjudicated against their frozen envelope per
345: references/frozen-plan-format.md, and only envelope overruns are drift) and,
346: for port work, **port fidelity** (drift from the reference
347: source), ending PASS / FIX / ESCALATE.
348: 
349: Backup lane: same protocol, transport and per-round evidence per references/backup-lane.md.
350: 
351: Panels: any reviewer-lane combination per references/panels.md.
352: 
353: A FIX this session applies itself goes through the **application
354: checkpoint** (references/application-checkpoint.md) before the first edit.
355: The fixed range is then re-reviewed, and only the post-re-review terminal
356: PASS is attested — with the emitter's `-CheckpointFile` binding the
357: checkpoint; a FIX verdict whose fixes are still unapplied is never the
358: attested verdict.
359: 
360: **Degraded-plan poisoning rule:** a plan whose Verification status is
361: DEGRADED cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS. Mode diff must first reopen
362: and cross-verify the plan's claims and approved deviations (the
363: retrospective pass the plan never got), and only then verify the
364: implementation against it — confirming a diff matches an unsound plan
365: verifies nothing. If cross-vendor verification is STILL unavailable, the
366: only terminal state is `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`: merging
367: then requires the user's explicitly recorded risk acceptance, never a PASS.
368: 
369: ## Finish line
370: 
371: Before it: the session's final-adjudication step (debate-protocol.md) —
372: verify the last round's findings against the repo and issue the terminal
373: verdict; a reviewer's PASS/FIX is never terminal by itself.
374: 
375: A PASS is terminal only for the exact head it was issued on. If you apply
376: anything the reviewer raised — including observations it labelled
377: non-blocking — the head moves and the verdict no longer covers it. Either
378: leave them for a follow-up branch, or run one confirming round.
379: 
380: **Mode diff only — record the verdict mechanically.** After the terminal
381: verdict, run the attestation emitter from this plugin's checkout:
382: 
383: ```powershell
384: powershell -NoProfile -File <plugin-root>/tools/write-attestation.ps1 -RepoRoot <reviewed-repo> -BaseSha <base> -HeadSha <head> -Verdict <PASS|FIX|ESCALATE> -VerificationStatus <FULL|DEGRADED> -RouteNote "<effective route confirmed | the transport-failure class>" -Rounds <n> -Participants "<session-model> (session) / <reviewer-model> (reviewer)" [-CheckpointFile <application-checkpoint-artifact>]
385: ```
386: 
387: When an application checkpoint governed fix application, pass it via
388: `-CheckpointFile`; references/application-checkpoint.md states what that
389: binds and which head it is bound to.
390: 
391: It writes `.git/parallax/attestations/<head-sha>.json` inside the reviewed
392: repo — untracked by design, so recording the verdict cannot move HEAD out
393: from under its own SHA. The pre-push lane (`tools/verify-attestation.ps1`)
394: later warns when a `main` push has no matching attestation for the pushed
395: head (fast-forward: pushed sha == attested head; merge commit: parent1 ==
396: attested base, parent2 == attested head — a squash changes the sha and
397: correctly forces re-review).
398: 
399: End with one status line: participating models, rounds used, converged vs
400: escalated points, the verification status — `FULL`, or
401: `DEGRADED (<class>, authorized by user at round N)` per fallbacks.md — and
402: the route note: `effective route confirmed` when every round's header
403: matched the canonical declarations, else the transport-failure class that
404: fallbacks.md handled. The route-note grammar is lane-agnostic: for a backup-lane debate,
405: `effective route confirmed` means every round satisfied
406: references/backup-lane.md's per-round evidence rules against the backup
407: declarations; the evidence class is recorded in the debate record prose. The session emits this line itself; never delegate it
408: to a subagent.
409: 
410: ## Common mistakes
411: 
412: - Accepting the reviewer's claims about reference code without the cited lines —
413:   strike the claim per protocol; do not argue against it.
414: - Re-sending the full debate context each round instead of resuming the
415:   codex session.
416: - Running mode `diff` against different SHAs than the code review used.
417: - Treating convergence as failure — a sound plan converging in one round is
418:   the system working, not a skipped debate.

4: are routinely gitignored, and a clone carries tracked files only. Probed

10: import json

12: import re
13: import shutil

15: import subprocess
16: import tempfile

19: import pytest
20: 
21: REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
22: MIRROR = REPO_ROOT / "tools" / "new-review-mirror.ps1"
23: FIXTURES = Path(__file__).parent / "fixtures" / "codex-prompt-input"
24: STUB = Path(__file__).parent / "fixtures" / "stub-codex" / "stub-codex.ps1"
25: 
26: 
27: # WINDOWS ONLY, for the same reason as the probe's suite: the mirror runs
28: # the probe, which resolves the reviewer's global instruction file under
29: # `$env:USERPROFILE`, and the lane lock lives under `$env:LOCALAPPDATA`.
30: # Neither exists on Linux, so the run refuses to report a measurement it
31: # could not make - the script working, not failing.
32: #
33: # Guarding only on a PowerShell host being present was not enough: the CI
34: # runner ships `pwsh`, so these cases ran on Linux and failed there for a
35: # second, different reason. See the probe suite's header for the full
36: # sequence.
37: #
38: # COVERAGE, recorded rather than assumed: these cases are Windows-only, so
39: # the ubuntu job skips them. The `powershell-hosts` job on windows-latest
40: # runs this module under BOTH powershell.exe and pwsh.exe. If this module
41: # ever leaves both Windows pytest steps, that claim is false and
42: # test_backup_lane.py asserts it back.
43: POWERSHELL = (os.environ.get("PARALLAX_PS_HOST")
44:               or shutil.which("powershell") or shutil.which("pwsh"))
45: 
46: pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(
47:     os.name != "nt" or POWERSHELL is None,
48:     reason="the review mirror is a Windows tool: it needs a PowerShell "
49:            "host and the Windows profile variables it measures")
50: 
51: 
52: def ps_host():
53:     return POWERSHELL
54: 
55: 
56: def git(repo, *args):
57:     return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), *args],
58:                           capture_output=True, text=True, check=True).stdout
59: 
60: 
61: def commit(repo, message):
62:     git(repo, "-c", "user.email=t@t", "-c", "user.name=t",
63:         "commit", "-q", "-m", message)
64: 
65: 
66: def make_repo(tmp_path):
67:     """A scratch repo with one tracked file, one ignored file, and one
68:     untracked file, so the baseline has something to carry."""
69:     repo = tmp_path / "src"
70:     repo.mkdir()
71:     git(tmp_path, "init", "-q", str(repo))
72:     (repo / "kept.txt").write_text("tracked\n")
73:     (repo / ".gitignore").write_text("ignored/\n")
74:     (repo / "ignored").mkdir()
75:     (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("gitignored input\n")
76:     (repo / "untracked.txt").write_text("untracked\n")
77:     git(repo, "add", "kept.txt", ".gitignore")
78:     commit(repo, "base")
79:     return repo
80: 
81: 
82: def make_clean_repo(tmp_path):
83:     """A repo with NOTHING for the baseline to carry: no back-channels, no
84:     untracked files, no ignored files. The ordinary case, and the one an
85:     empty-array return would have misread as an enumeration failure."""
86:     repo = tmp_path / "clean"
87:     repo.mkdir()
88:     git(tmp_path, "init", "-q", str(repo))
89:     (repo / "only.txt").write_text("tracked\n")
90:     git(repo, "add", "only.txt")
91:     commit(repo, "base")
92:     return repo
93: 
94: 
95: def run_mirror(repo, mirror, *extra, env=None):
96:     """`env` carries the build's three TEST SEAMS.
97: 
98:     They are environment variables, not parameters, for the reason the
99:     lane-home builder states about its own two: a parameter is public
100:     surface on a shipped tool. No shipped caller sets either.
101: 
102:     Each is a BOOLEAN that ORs one extra block condition into a
103:     comparison. It cannot supply a value, so it cannot suppress a real
104:     measurement, and setting it can only ever ADD a reason to fail.
105:     That is what makes the one-way claim structural rather than a
106:     property of the value someone happens to pass.
107: 
108:     Two earlier shapes failed that bar and the whole-branch review
109:     caught both. The first copied from a different tree and committed
110:     into the real source repo. The second INJECTED a value, so a parent
111:     that set the variable to the repo's own current HEAD - trivially
112:     readable in advance - suppressed the genuine post-copy measurement
113:     and turned a build that should have failed into one that passed.
114:     """
115:     full = None
116:     if env:
117:         full = dict(os.environ)
118:         full.update(env)
119:     return subprocess.run(
120:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(MIRROR),
121:          "-RepoRoot", str(repo), "-MirrorPath", str(mirror),
122:          "-SkipProbe", *extra],
123:         capture_output=True, text=True, env=full)
124: 
125: 
126: SKIP_BLOCK = "BLOCKED: no client measurement was made (-SkipProbe)"
127: 
128: 
129: def assert_built(proc):
130:     """-SkipProbe builds the mirror but VERIFIES nothing, so it exits 1.
131: 
132:     The exit code reports dispatch-readiness, not construction: a mirror
133:     with no client measurement is not cleared for dispatch, and must not
134:     share an exit code with one that is. The mode-diff review of
135:     2026-07-28 found the old exit 0 there.
136: 
137:     Asserting the SPECIFIC skip line is what keeps these tests able to
138:     catch a real construction failure, which also exits 1 but with a
139:     different reason.
140:     """
141:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
142:     assert SKIP_BLOCK in proc.stdout, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
143: 
144: 
145: def make_junction(link, target):
146:     """A junction via mklink /J, or skip. Junctions need no privilege;
147:     symbolic links do, and this session cannot make one."""
148:     rc = subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "mklink", "/J", str(link), str(target)],
149:                         capture_output=True, text=True)
150:     if rc.returncode != 0:
151:         pytest.skip("junction creation unavailable: " + rc.stderr)
152: 
153: 
154: def unlink_junction(link):
155:     """rmdir removes the junction and never its target."""
156:     subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "rmdir", str(link)],
157:                    capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
158: 
159: 
160: def is_reparse_point(path):
161:     """Python's is_symlink() is False for a junction, so read the
162:     attribute bit the filesystem sets for both kinds of link."""
163:     return bool(os.lstat(str(path)).st_file_attributes
164:                 & stat.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT)
165: 
166: 
167: def read_block(stdout, label):
168:     """Lines of one labelled block from the record output."""
169:     lines = stdout.splitlines()
170:     start = next(i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if line.startswith(label))
171:     out = []
172:     for line in lines[start + 1:]:
173:         if line and not line.startswith("  "):
174:             break
175:         if line.strip():
176:             out.append(line.strip())
177:     return out

180: def test_the_mirror_carries_gitignored_files(tmp_path):
181:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
182:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
183:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
184:     assert_built(proc)
185:     assert (mirror / "ignored" / "secret.txt").exists(), (
186:         "a clone would have dropped this; the mirror must not"
187:     )
188:     assert (mirror / ".git").exists()

191: def test_a_tracked_agents_md_is_deleted_and_committed(tmp_path):
192:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
193:     (repo / "AGENTS.md").write_text("# planted\n")
194:     git(repo, "add", "AGENTS.md")
195:     commit(repo, "plant")
196:     before = git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
197:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
198:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
199:     assert_built(proc)
200:     assert not (mirror / "AGENTS.md").exists()
201:     assert (repo / "AGENTS.md").exists(), "the real tree is never touched"
202:     after = git(mirror, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
203:     assert after != before, (
204:         "a tracked deletion left uncommitted is a tracked modification in"
205:         " the baseline, which bars mode diff and breaks"
206:         " HEAD-identifies-content"
207:     )
208:     assert "AGENTS.md" not in git(mirror, "status", "--porcelain")

211: def test_an_ignored_agents_drop_is_deleted_without_a_commit(tmp_path):
212:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
213:     (repo / ".gitignore").write_text("ignored/\n.agents/\n")
214:     git(repo, "add", ".gitignore")
215:     commit(repo, "ignore agents")
216:     skill = repo / ".agents" / "skills" / "planted"
217:     skill.mkdir(parents=True)
218:     (skill / "SKILL.md").write_text("---\nname: planted\n---\n")
219:     before = git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
220:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
221:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
222:     assert_built(proc)
223:     assert not (mirror / ".agents").exists()
224:     after = git(mirror, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
225:     assert after == before, (
226:         "nothing to commit alongside an unchanged HEAD is the CORRECT"
227:         " observation for an ignored entry, not an inconsistency to chase"
228:     )

231: def test_a_gitignored_back_channel_is_found_and_removed(tmp_path):
232:     # Checked 2026-07-28 against a claim that the enumeration misses
233:     # ignored files: it does not. `--others` WITHOUT `--exclude-standard`
234:     # lists ignored files too, and a gitignored root AGENTS.md IS ingested
235:     # by codex.
236:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
237:     (repo / ".gitignore").write_text("ignored/\n.agents/\nAGENTS.md\n")
238:     git(repo, "add", ".gitignore")
239:     commit(repo, "ignore the back-channels")
240:     (repo / "AGENTS.md").write_text("# ignored but still ingested\n")
241:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
242:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
243:     assert_built(proc)
244:     assert not (mirror / "AGENTS.md").exists()

261: def test_a_nested_dot_agents_is_a_recorded_gap_not_a_silent_one(tmp_path):
262:     # `.agents/*` is anchored at the repo root, so a nested drop is NOT
263:     # enumerated. Measured 2026-07-28: codex-cli 0.144.1 advertises a ROOT
264:     # .agents/skills entry and does NOT advertise a nested one, so this is
265:     # unreachable today. The client probe covers it regardless. This test
266:     # records the boundary so a future change turns it red instead of
267:     # passing silently.
268:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
269:     deep = repo / "sub" / ".agents" / "skills" / "deep"
270:     deep.mkdir(parents=True)
271:     (deep / "SKILL.md").write_text("---\nname: deep\n---\n")
272:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
273:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
274:     assert_built(proc)
275:     assert (mirror / "sub" / ".agents" / "skills" / "deep" / "SKILL.md").exists(), (
276:         "the root-anchored pathspec does not reach this entry; if this ever"
277:         " starts being removed, the enumeration changed and the accepted"
278:         " limit in the design must be updated in the same commit"
279:     )

1504: # proves the mirror was built FROM the recorded source commit - two true
1505: # facts arranged to look like one.
1506: #
1507: # THE CLAIM IS NARROW, deliberately: the two-HEAD gate proves
1508: # committed-HEAD freshness. Non-HEAD inputs are bound in the manifest at
1509: # CONSTRUCTION TIME, and source-side changes after construction are
1510: # caught by the source-status comparison, not by the HEADs. An edit to an

1516: def _force_remove(func, path, _exc):
1517:     """git marks its object files read-only, so a plain rmtree of a
1518:     mirror raises PermissionError on Windows before it reaches the
1519:     condition under test."""
1520:     os.chmod(path, 0o700)

1524: def record_field(stdout, name):
1525:     """One scalar field of the construction record, or None."""
1526:     for line in stdout.splitlines():
1527:         if line.startswith(name + ": "):
1528:             return line[len(name) + 2:].strip()
1529:     return None

1532: def build_and_read(repo, mirror, *extra):
1533:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror, *extra)
1534:     assert_built(proc)
1535:     return proc, {
1536:         "source_head": record_field(proc.stdout, "source_head"),
1537:         "mirror_head": record_field(proc.stdout, "mirror_head"),
1538:         "source_status_sha256": record_field(proc.stdout,
1539:                                              "source_status_sha256"),
1540:         "mirror_state_sha256": record_field(proc.stdout,
1541:                                             "mirror_state_sha256"),
1542:         "mirror": record_field(proc.stdout, "mirror"),
1543:     }

1546: def run_verify(repo, mirror, ident, **override):
1547:     """`mirror` is BOTH the live -MirrorPath argument (2nd positional)
1548:     AND, by default, -ExpectedMirrorPath - the path the identity was
1549:     recorded at. Pass `mirror=` in `override` to point the two at
1550:     different places on purpose (test_verify_refuses_a_mirror_at_a_
1551:     different_path)."""
1552:     fields = dict(ident)
1553:     fields.update(override)
1554:     return subprocess.run(
1555:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(MIRROR),
1556:          "-VerifyIdentity", "-RepoRoot", str(repo), "-MirrorPath", str(mirror),
1557:          "-ExpectedMirrorPath", str(fields["mirror"]),
1558:          "-SourceHead", str(fields["source_head"]),
1559:          "-MirrorHead", str(fields["mirror_head"]),
1560:          "-SourceStatusSha256", str(fields["source_status_sha256"]),
1561:          "-MirrorStateSha256", str(fields["mirror_state_sha256"])],
1562:         capture_output=True, text=True)

1567: def test_the_record_carries_both_heads_and_the_source_status_hash(tmp_path):
1568:     """The positive control for construction.
1569: 
1570:     `mirror_head` differs from `source_head` whenever remediation
1571:     committed, which is the ordinary case for a repo carrying a tracked
1572:     back-channel - so the two fields are not interchangeable and a
1573:     record printing one twice would be wrong in the common case.
1574:     """
1575:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
1576:     proc, ident = build_and_read(repo, tmp_path / "mirror")
1577:     assert ident["source_head"], proc.stdout
1578:     assert ident["mirror_head"], proc.stdout
1579:     assert len(ident["source_status_sha256"]) == 64, proc.stdout
1580:     assert ident["source_head"] == git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
1581:

1590:         "remediation committed, so the two identities must differ; a "

105: function Write-SourceManifestSidecar($path, $manifestLines) {
106:     # ADVISORY ONLY. It pins nothing and gates nothing, so a failure to
107:     # write it is not a build failure - it costs a later refusal its
108:     # explanation and nothing else. That is also why it may be a file at
109:     # all: the header's rule about values passed as arguments rather than
110:     # re-read from a file governs values that PIN something, and this
111:     # value carries no authority.
112:     try {
113:         $utf8 = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
114:         [System.IO.File]::WriteAllLines($path, [string[]]@($manifestLines), $utf8)
115:         return $true
116:     } catch {
117:         return $false
118:     }
119: }
120: ```

265: - [ ] **Step 3: Add the diff function**
266: 
267: Insert into `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` immediately after `Write-SourceManifestSidecar`:
268: 
269: ```powershell
270: function Get-ManifestDrift($recordedLines, $liveLines) {
271:     # Both sides are "<relpath> <sha256hex>" lines, so the LAST space is
272:     # the separator: a pathname may hold spaces and a hex digest may not.
273:     #
274:     # A line this parser cannot read is DROPPED rather than raised. This
275:     # function runs only on a path that is already refusing, and a bad
276:     # explanation must never replace a correct refusal with an error.
277:     $recorded = @{}
278:     foreach ($line in @($recordedLines)) {
279:         $cut = ([string]$line).LastIndexOf(" ")
280:         if ($cut -lt 1) { continue }
281:         $recorded[([string]$line).Substring(0, $cut)] = ([string]$line).Substring($cut + 1)
282:     }
283:     $live = @{}
284:     foreach ($line in @($liveLines)) {
285:         $cut = ([string]$line).LastIndexOf(" ")
286:         if ($cut -lt 1) { continue }
287:         $live[([string]$line).Substring(0, $cut)] = ([string]$line).Substring($cut + 1)
288:     }
289:     $appeared = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
290:     $vanished = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
291:     $changed = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
292:     foreach ($p in @($live.Keys)) {
293:         if (-not $recorded.ContainsKey($p)) {
294:             [void]$appeared.Add($p)
295:         } elseif ($recorded[$p] -ne $live[$p]) {
296:             [void]$changed.Add($p)
297:         }
298:     }
299:     foreach ($p in @($recorded.Keys)) {
300:         if (-not $live.ContainsKey($p)) { [void]$vanished.Add($p) }
301:     }
302:     return @{ Appeared = @($appeared | Sort-Object)
303:               Vanished = @($vanished | Sort-Object)
304:               Changed  = @($changed  | Sort-Object) }
305: }
306: ```
307: 
308: - [ ] **Step 4: Add the explanation function**
309: 
310: Insert immediately after `Get-ManifestDrift`:
311: 
312: ```powershell
313: function Write-SourceDriftExplanation($mirrorPath, $liveManifest) {
314:     # Runs ONLY after the source-status refusal below has been printed.
315:     # It prints what moved, or says plainly that it could not tell. It
316:     # never throws, never exits, and never returns a value a caller
317:     # could branch on, so no state of the sidecar can change a verdict.
318:     $sidecar = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $mirrorPath
319:     $recorded = $null
320:     try {
321:         if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $sidecar -PathType Leaf) {
322:             $recorded = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllLines($sidecar,
323:                 (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)))
324:         }
325:     } catch {
326:         $recorded = $null
327:     }
328:     if ($null -eq $recorded) {
329:         Write-Output ("  what moved: unknown - no readable source manifest" +
330:             " at " + $sidecar)
331:         return
332:     }
333:     $drift = Get-ManifestDrift $recorded $liveManifest
334:     $groups = @(@("changed", $drift.Changed),
335:                 @("appeared", $drift.Appeared),
336:                 @("vanished", $drift.Vanished))
337:     $any = $false
338:     foreach ($g in $groups) {
339:         $names = @($g[1])
340:         if ($names.Count -eq 0) { continue }
341:         $any = $true
342:         Write-Output ("  " + $g[0] + " (" + $names.Count + "):")
343:         $shown = 0
344:         foreach ($n in $names) {
345:             if ($shown -ge 20) {
346:                 Write-Output ("    ... and " + ($names.Count - 20) + " more")
347:                 break
348:             }
349:             Write-Output ("    " + $n)
350:             $shown++
351:         }
352:     }
353:     if (-not $any) {
354:         Write-Output ("  what moved: no content difference, so the status" +
355:             " listing itself changed - a path took a different status" +
356:             " code, or a deletion-only entry moved. Those carry no bytes" +
357:             " and so appear in no manifest.")
358:     }
359: }
360: ```
361:

368: ```

522: - [ ] **Step 3: Add the timing rule**
523: 
524: Append to `skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md`, as a new final section:
525: 
526: ```markdown
527: ## Timing
528: 
529: BUILD THE MIRROR LAST. Every act that writes inside the reviewed
530: repository finishes first: the gates, the plan ledger, the scratch notes,
531: the formatter. From the build until the round's wrapper exits, the
532: repository is quiet, and SKILL.md's `mirror-quiet-period` region states
533: why. The identity digest covers the content of ignored paths, so a
573: `git status --porcelain --ignored` names, and `Get-ContentManifest`
574: expands a directory subject recursively, so one `!! .claude/` entry
575: pulls in every file beneath it. The content half is deliberate and
576: load-bearing: editing an already-ignored file leaves the status listing
577: byte-identical, so a list-only digest would pass through exactly the
578: tampering the check exists to catch.
579: 
580: The exposure is the WHOLE ROUND, not the preparation gap. The recorded
581: digest is compared three times, and the third comparison runs after the
582: client finishes. In a repository whose ignored directories move on their
583: own, no ordering discipline closes that.
584: 
585: **What this cycle did and did not do.** The refusal now names the paths
586: that moved, the skill states the quiet period, and the reference orders
587: the build last. None of that narrows the digest, so the underlying
588: exposure is unchanged and this item stays OPEN.
589: 
590: **A candidate fix, rejected for now.** A declared allowlist of volatile
591: ignored paths held outside the content digest. Rejected because the
592: directories that move on their own, `.claude/` and `.codex/`, are
593: instruction surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the
594: tamper net defeats the check. Closing this item means a design that
595: narrows the digest without opening that hole, argued in the
596: `mirror-identity-gate` contract region and debated on its own.
597: ```

1: # Preflight: building the review mirror
2: 
3: Construction detail for SKILL.md's preflight item 3, "build the review
4: mirror" step. This is read only when the enumeration there actually found
5: an `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/*`, or `.kimi-code/*` entry and
6: SKILL.md's back-channel-auto-mirror requires the mirror — the operational
7: imperative and the do-not-ask rule stay in SKILL.md itself; this is the
8: how, not the whether.
9: 
10: Run
11: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1 -RepoRoot <repo> -MirrorPath <scratch>`.
12: Build at a SHORT `<scratch>` directly under the temp directory, such
13: as a `kerev<n>` folder, never inside the session scratchpad: the
14: mirror re-roots every path, and the tool refuses before creating
15: anything when the budget is blown.
16: It builds the **review mirror** (references/backup-lane.md owns its
17: construction, its baseline, and its identity fields — a file copy
18: preserving `.git`, NOT a clone), deletes the offending entries THERE,
19: commits when any were tracked, re-runs the enumeration from SKILL.md's
20: preflight item 3 inside the mirror, captures the baseline and the
21: content manifest, runs the client probe from that same preflight step
22: with the mirror as the working directory, and prints the record block;
23: empty enumeration output is the evidence, and the mirror's identity
24: fields go in the debate record. The mirror is then the reviewed tree for
25: every lane in that debate — dispatch codex with the mirror as cwd, and
26: keep citations resolvable in the real repo. Whether the removal needs a
27: commit branches on tracked-ness, and the difference misreads as a
28: failure; references/backup-lane.md states that branch and the hook
29: behaviour that comes with it.
30: 
31: Files above the repo's git root are NOT ingested (same probe), and
32: `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` is the user's own
33: global instruction file — note it in the debate record if it exists,
34: but it is not a stop.
33:    The plugin therefore does not take the rule to the repos it is
34:    installed into.
35: 
36: ## What the reporting session proposed, and why it is not the fix
37: 
38: The report proposed fusing the mirror build and `-Prepare` into one
39: command. That is refused on two grounds:
40: 
41: - It closes the first of three windows and leaves the largest one open. A
42:   round that takes ten minutes still voids if an ignored file moves while
43:   the reviewer reads.
44: - It is not constructible. The build runs the client context probe, the
45:   probe writes the verified override file, and the wrapper body embeds
46:   that file's SHA256 (`skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md`, the
47:   `verified-override-dispatch` region). The build must finish before the
48:   wrapper body can exist, and the wrapper body must exist before
49:   `-Prepare` can install it.
50: 
51: The real constraint is a QUIET PERIOD over the reviewed repository, from
52: the build until the wrapper exits.
53: 
54: ## Decisions
55: 
56: **D1. The gate does not change.** No path is removed from the digest, no
57: carve-out is added, and no re-mint or reseal mode is introduced. A
58: volatile-path allowlist was considered and rejected for this cycle: the
59: directories that move on their own (`.claude/`, `.codex/`) are instruction
60: surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the tamper net defeats
61: the check. It is recorded as an open backlog item, not built.
62: 
63: **D2. The refusal explains itself, advisorily.** The build writes the
64: SOURCE side's content manifest to a SIBLING of the mirror directory,
65: `<MirrorPath>.source-manifest`. On the source-status refusal path only,
66: `-VerifyIdentity` reads that file and names the paths that appeared,
67: vanished, or changed content.
68: 
69: The advisory file is safe by construction and must stay that way:
70: 
71: - It is read ONLY after the comparison has already decided to block, so
72:   it can never turn a refusal into a pass. A missing, unreadable,
73:   truncated, or deliberately corrupted file changes the printed
74:   explanation and never the exit code.
75: - It is a sibling, not a child, of the mirror and lives outside the
76:   repository, so it enters neither `source_status_sha256` nor
77:   `mirror_state_sha256`.
78: - It pins nothing, which is why it may be a file at all. The header's
79:   rule that identity values are passed as arguments rather than re-read
80:   from a file the build wrote applies to values that PIN something; this
81:   one carries no authority.
82: 
83: **D3. The quiet period ships in the skill.** A contract region states it,
84: covering build through wrapper exit, so it travels with the plugin.
85: 
86: **D4. The build is ordered last.** `references/preflight-mirror.md` gains

592:   <!-- contract:end -->
593:   <!-- contract:start id=round-dispatch-exit-map -->
594:   `-Classify`'s exit code is the whole verdict: 0 means reply-present
595:   and nothing else; 2 means a parameter-binding failure, an unrecognized
596:   argument, or an internal execution error; 1 means every other state,
597:   named on the wrapper's last stdout line. The wrapper's own last
598:   statement is `exit $LASTEXITCODE` after calling `-Classify` as its
599:   last act, so the wrapper's exit code IS the classification. A caller
600:   reads the exit code of the harness task it dispatched, and never opens
601:   the dispatch directory for a verdict.
602:   <!-- contract:end -->
603:   <!-- contract:start id=round-dispatch-operation -->
604:   There is NO POLL. The caller dispatches the wrapper as a harness
605:   background task and then WAITS for the harness notification for that
606:   exact task; nothing in this tool watches a directory for the caller. A
607:   round with no notification is UNFINISHED, never successful. A SESSION
608:   MUST NEVER END ITS TURN WITH A DISPATCHED ROUND UNFINISHED. In an
609:   interactive session the notification opens a new turn, so stopping is
610:   correct. In a print-mode or otherwise unattended run the turn a
611:   session ends is its last, so stopping there ends the run with the
612:   round still in flight and no verdict at all - measured 2026-09-01,
613:   where a graded run dispatched its round as a background task, said
614:   it would wait, and ended its turn with no verdict. Where no notification can reach the session,
615:   it WAITS on that exact task through the harness's own task-output
616:   read, which is still the harness surface and not a directory poll.
617:   Where it can do neither, it finishes as a TRANSPORT FAILURE, never
618:   with a verdict-less finish line. Recovery
619:   is a FRESH `-Prepare` with a fresh evidence boundary, never a re-run
620:   of the same wrapper: the claim and classification files are reserved
621:   create-new on the first run, so the wrapper itself refuses a second
622:   run rather than retrying. To abandon a round, kill the harness task.
623:   Never poll with `ps -p` from Git Bash, which cannot see Windows pids
624:   and reports a live process as gone.
625:   <!-- contract:end -->
626:   <!-- contract:start id=background-task-naming -->
627:   Name the backgrounded call for the person watching it. The reviewer
628:   LANE and the ROUND lead the description, as in `Astra R1 debate round`
629:   or `Kimi R2 debate round`; work with no lane leads with its kind, as
630:   in `Gate: pytest 5.1` or `Mirror build`. A cycle runs several lanes

700:   fallbacks.md never names models.
701: - **Structured quota state is locally readable (experimental)**: probed
702:   2026-07-24 (codex-cli 0.144.1): `codex app-server --stdio` with an
687:   remediation, the re-enumeration, the baseline, the manifest and the
688:   client probe as one step; the rules below remain its specification, and
689:   a driver building a mirror by hand still follows them.
690: - **Whether the removal needs a commit branches on tracked-ness, and the
691:   difference misreads as a failure.**
692:   a TRACKED entry's deletion shows as ` D` in `git status --porcelain`
693:   — a tracked MODIFICATION, which the post-remediation baseline would
694:   absorb, so the per-round check is not what forces the issue: an
695:   uncommitted ` D` leaves a tracked modification sitting in the
696:   baseline, which bars mode diff and breaks HEAD-identifies-content
697:   — so commit the removal
698:   inside the mirror; an IGNORED or untracked entry's deletion shows
699:   nothing, no commit is possible, and HEAD legitimately stays where it
700:   was — `nothing to commit` alongside an unchanged HEAD is the CORRECT
701:   observation there, not an inconsistency to chase (both observed
702:   2026-07-26). The mirror carries the real repo's `.git`, hooks
703:   included, so a commit inside it runs that project's pre-commit hooks
704:   against the scratchpad copy: expect them to fire, and treat a hook
705:   failure as a mirror-construction problem, never as a finding about
706:   the reviewed work.
707: - **Mirror identity, and the gate that keeps it fresh.**
708:   <!-- contract:start id=mirror-identity-gate -->
709:   The record carries TWO identities, TWO fingerprints, and the path it
710:   was built at: `source_head`, `mirror_head`, `source_status_sha256`,
711:   `mirror_state_sha256`, and the mirror's own recorded path. The two
712:   heads differ whenever remediation committed, which is the ordinary
713:   case for a repo carrying a tracked back-channel, so a record printing
714:   one of them twice is wrong in the common case rather than the rare
715:   one. Construction is a
716:   six-step bridge. Capture the source head BEFORE the copy; copy;
717:   require the live source head still equals it; before remediation,
718:   require the COPIED tree's head equals it; remediate, then record
719:   `mirror_head`. Steps three and four are the bridge itself: without
720:   them the record can hold two individually valid commit ids while
721:   nothing proves the mirror was built FROM the recorded source commit,
722:   which is two true facts arranged to look like one. What the bridge
723:   proves is matching OBSERVED ENDPOINTS, and that is weaker than an
724:   uninterrupted construction: a source that moves away and back during
725:   the copy satisfies both the before-and-after head equality and the
726:   before-and-after fingerprint, while the copied worktree can still hold
727:   intermediate bytes. The debate named that gap and it is real; the only
728:   thing that would close it is building from an immutable snapshot,
729:   which this release does not do. Before every fresh
730:   and resumed dispatch, re-run the tool with `-VerifyIdentity` and the
731:   five recorded values. Missing, unreadable or unequal BLOCKS the
732:   round, and a value that was never recorded is never a value that
733:   matched. Two refusals guard the comparison itself, both ahead of any
734:   digest work: the source and the mirror must not be the same
735:   directory — otherwise every remaining comparison is trivially
736:   satisfied whenever the two heads already agree, which they do
737:   whenever the mirror needed no remediation commit — and the live
738:   `-MirrorPath` must canonically match `-ExpectedMirrorPath`. Both are
739:   CALLER ARGUMENTS in the SAME invocation rather than values read back
740:   from the construction record, so the guarantee is narrow and is
741:   stated narrowly: it catches a verify whose two path arguments
742:   disagree, and it does NOT refuse a mirror rebuilt somewhere else with
743:   both arguments updated to the new path. What the
744:   gate proves is narrow and stated so: the two-HEAD
745:   gate proves committed-HEAD freshness. Non-HEAD inputs are bound in the
746:   constructed mirror's manifest AT CONSTRUCTION TIME, and source-side
747:   changes after construction are detected by the source-status
748:   comparison below WHEN THEY ARE VISIBLE TO IT: that is, changes that
749:   move the status listing, or that alter the content of a path the
750:   listing names. Changes made directly INSIDE the mirror after
751:   construction — an edit to a file the mirror's own HEAD still calls
1: # Mirror Identity Window Implementation Plan
2: 
3: > **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4: 
5: **Goal:** Make the review mirror's identity refusal name the paths that moved, and ship the quiet-period rule that the refusal enforces, so a session outside this repo can act on both.
6: 
7: **Architecture:** Three independent changes and no change to the gate. The build writes the source side's content manifest to a sibling of the mirror directory; the source-status refusal reads that sibling, on that path only, to name what appeared, vanished, or changed content; and the skill grows a contract region plus a timing rule stating that nothing may write inside the reviewed repository between the build and the wrapper's exit. The sidecar file is advisory: it is read after the comparison has already decided to block, so no state it can be in changes any exit code.
8: 
9: **Tech Stack:** Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1`), Python 3 with pytest (`evals/multi-model-verify/`), Markdown contract regions under `skills/`.
10: 
11: **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md`
12: 
13: ## Global Constraints
14: 
15: - `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` is **Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible and ASCII ONLY**. No smart quotes, no dashes outside the ASCII hyphen, no non-ASCII byte anywhere in the file.
16: - **No existing exit code may change in any existing case.** The gate stays fail-closed. Every new code path may only ADD printed output on a path that is already exiting 1.
17: - **Tests first.** Write the failing test, run it, watch it fail for the stated reason, then implement.
18: - **Do not bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` in this plan.** The bump happens after the diff debate, per `CLAUDE.md`.
19: - **Do not run pytest, the gates, or any repo-writing command while a review round is in flight.** That is the defect this plan documents.
20: - A new contract region needs BOTH a whole-region pin in `evals/multi-model-verify/` AND an entry in `DECLARED_REGIONS` in `evals/multi-model-verify/test_contract_coverage.py`.
21: - A pin asserted with `in text` against the raw file matches **line breaks and leading indentation exactly**. Write the Markdown first, then copy those lines byte for byte into the pin.
22: - Commits are lowercase imperative with no AI attribution. **A commit message must not contain a bare `-Word` token**: the family git guard reads it as a passed flag and denies the commit (backlog item 79).
23: - Work on a feature branch, never on `main`.
24:

31: - Test: `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py`
209:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
210:     _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
211:     (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").unlink()
212:     proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
213:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
214:     assert "vanished" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout

218: def test_a_missing_source_manifest_still_refuses(tmp_path):
219:     """The explanation is ADVISORY. Its absence costs the reason and
220:     never the refusal."""
221:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
222:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
223:     _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)

225:     (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("edited after the copy\n")
226:     proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
227:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
228:     assert "source status" in proc.stdout.lower(), proc.stdout
229:     assert "unknown" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout

231: 
232: def test_a_corrupted_source_manifest_still_refuses(tmp_path):
233:     """The direction that matters. A sidecar an attacker can write must
234:     never turn a refusal into a pass, and it structurally cannot: it is
235:     read only after the comparison has already decided to block."""
236:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
237:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
238:     _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
239:     (tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest").write_bytes(b"\x00garbage\xff\nno")
240:     (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("edited after the copy\n")
241:     proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
242:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
243:     assert "source status" in proc.stdout.lower(), proc.stdout
244: 
245: 
246: def test_a_clean_tree_verifies_with_no_source_manifest(tmp_path):
247:     """The other advisory direction. A tree that did not move verifies
248:     whether or not the sidecar survives, because the sidecar is not an
249:     input to the comparison."""
250:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
251:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
252:     _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
253:     (tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest").unlink()
254:     proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
255:     assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
256:     assert "identity: verified" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout

259: - [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and confirm they fail for the stated reason**
414: ```markdown
415:    <!-- contract:start id=mirror-quiet-period -->
416:    NOTHING MAY WRITE INSIDE THE REVIEWED REPOSITORY from the moment the
417:    mirror is built until the wrapper exits. The identity digest covers the
418:    CONTENT of every path `git status --porcelain --ignored` names, ignored
419:    ones included, so a test-cache write, a plan-ledger append, a drift
420:    report or one new untracked file is enough. The same recorded digest is
421:    compared against the live source three times: at preparation, before the
422:    client runs, and after it finishes. The last of those spans the whole
423:    round, so this is a quiet period and not an ordering rule. A round that
424:    trips it is refused, its reply is NOT evidence, and the quota is spent
425:    for nothing. Queue every edit until the wrapper exits, however small and
426:    however unrelated it looks.
427:    <!-- contract:end -->
428: ```
429: 
430: - [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing pin**
431: 
432: Add to `evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py`, in `TestSkillStructure` directly after `test_client_context_probe_failure_rule_is_pinned`:
433: 
535: dispatch.
536: 
537: A build that has gone stale is not repaired and cannot be re-blessed:
538: there is deliberately no re-mint or reseal mode. Build again. It is
539: cheap next to a spent round, measured at about 92 seconds on a repo
540: carrying a linked reference checkout.
541: 
542: When a refusal names `the source status changed since construction`, the
543: lines beneath it name the paths that changed, appeared or vanished,
544: read from the `source_manifest` file the record block points at. That
545: explanation is advisory: it can be missing or wrong and the refusal
546: stands either way.

18: #
19: # This script never writes to the real tree, never dispatches a review, and
20: # never decides to proceed. It stops immediately before the brief is
21: # written, because the brief is the first artifact that is not evidence.
22: #
23: # Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible, ASCII ONLY.
24: #
25: # Exit codes: 0 built and clean, 1 blocked (reason on stdout), 2 script or
26: # environment error.
27: # TWO MODES. Build constructs the mirror and prints the record. Verify
1423: $found = Get-BackChannelEntry $MirrorPath
1424: if (-not $found.Ok) {
1425:     Write-Output ("ERROR: could not enumerate back-channels in the" +
1426:         " mirror: " + $found.Reason)
1427:     exit 2
1428: }
1429: $entries = $found.Entries
1430: $tracked = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
1431: foreach ($entry in $entries) {
1432:     if (Test-Tracked $MirrorPath $entry) { [void]$tracked.Add($entry) }
1433:     $full = Join-Path $MirrorPath $entry
1434:     if (Test-Path $full) { Remove-Item -LiteralPath $full -Recurse -Force }
1435:     # Prune the directories the entry lived in, up to but never including
1436:     # the mirror root. `git ls-files` names FILES, so deleting one leaves
1437:     # `.agents/skills/<name>/` standing - an empty shell of the very
1438:     # surface this step exists to remove.
1439:     $dir = Split-Path (Join-Path $MirrorPath $entry) -Parent
1440:     while ($dir -and ($dir.Length -gt $MirrorPath.Length) -and
1441:            (Test-Path $dir) -and
1442:            -not (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $dir -Force)) {
1443:         Remove-Item -LiteralPath $dir -Force
1444:         $dir = Split-Path $dir -Parent
1445:     }
1446: }
1447: if ($tracked.Count -gt 0) {
1448:     # Suppress repository hooks for the remediation commit BELOW. The
1449:     # mirror carries the real repo's .git, hooks included (see the
1450:     # BLOCKED text on the commit call), so committing here without
1451:     # redirecting core.hooksPath would run whatever hook the reviewed
1452:     # repo ships. The directory lives inside the MIRROR's own .git,
1453:     # where a plain filesystem copy carries it along for free but
1454:     # `git status`/`ls-files` never see it, so it cannot register as a
1455:     # back-channel or dirty the porcelain itself.
1456:     #
1518: # COPY EACH -ExtraInput IN NOW, as part of construction and BEFORE the
1519: # baseline and the manifest are taken below, so the identity record
1520: # covers it and the printed record enumerates it - it lands as an
1521: # ordinary untracked mirror file, the same as any other review input the
1522: # mirror cannot inherit on its own.
1523: # Two refusals BEFORE any of them is copied, so a rejected build never
1524: # leaves half the inputs in place. Every input is flattened to its leaf
1525: # name, because the mirror re-roots every path; that makes two different
1526: # files with one leaf name indistinguishable at the destination, and it
1527: # lets an outside file land on a name the copied tree already carries.
1528: # Cross-vendor round 2 found both: the loop wrote with -Force, so the
1529: # second of a colliding pair silently won and a tracked file could be
1530: # replaced by unreviewed content that the digest then certified. The
1550:             " digest would then certify")
1551:         exit 1
1552:     }
1553: }

1. The digest includes ignored-file contents. `Get-BaselineRaw` includes ignored paths, `Get-StatusSha256` incorporates their content manifest, and directory subjects expand recursively before hashing. The ignored-file drift test exercises precisely this behavior. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:437`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:595`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:624`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:689`; `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:1658`.) **PASS.**

2. The content component is deliberate and necessary for the stated ignored-file protection. The implementation explicitly explains why unchanged status text cannot detect an edit to an existing ignored file, and the test requires that edit to refuse verification. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:671`; `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:1666`.) **PASS.**

3. All three checks use the recorded source digest, including the check after the client returns. Thus preparation alone does not end the exposure. Precisely, the final measurement occurs before classification and wrapper exit; “until wrapper exit” is a conservative operational boundary. The implementation explicitly limits detection to mutations that persist until measurement. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:458`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:274`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:305`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:335`.) **PASS.**

4. The dependency establishes sequencing, not impossibility. One command could complete the build and probe, generate the body using the reported override hash, then invoke preparation. That sequence satisfies every cited dependency. The design’s separate objection—that fusion leaves the client’s running time exposed—stands. Also, preparation copies the body at line 530; lines 264–267 belong to the generated wrapper template. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1725`; `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:184`; `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:530`; [design:41](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md:41).) **FIX: replace “not constructible” with “does not solve the exposure during the round.”**

5. The inserted refusal hook does preserve the already-selected refusal in its ordinary execution: its caller proceeds directly to `exit 1`. The broader assertion about the remedy fails at construction. A pre-existing sidecar hard link to an ignored source file would be overwritten by `WriteAllLines` after source measurement. Subsequent verification changes from success to refusal because the diagnostic writer changed the source itself. Existing destination guards cover the mirror and override, leaving the proposed sidecar unchecked. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:845`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1258`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1409`; [plan:114](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:114), [plan:124](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:124), [plan:364](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:364).) **FIX: make sidecar creation incapable of overwriting existing files or aliases, and test construction-side effects.**

6. An ordinary sibling file sits outside both directory trees, but its spelling does not establish filesystem independence. The hard-link case above makes its bytes reachable through a source manifest path. The existing protections explicitly account for aliases for other destinations; the proposed destination receives none of those checks. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1215`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1258`; [plan:96](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:96), [plan:114](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:114).) **FIX: establish destination independence through safe creation and collision handling; sibling placement alone is insufficient.**

7. The header rule concerns mutable files redefining expected identity values. The proposed reader supplies diagnostic text after digest inequality, without supplying an expected digest. That use fits the rule’s stated scope. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:49`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:845`; [plan:367](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:367).) **PASS.**

8. The repository rule explicitly covers preparation through wrapper exit and explicitly says no skill prose carries it. The skill’s build and dispatch instructions contain no equivalent quiet-period requirement; the reference describes construction without that timing constraint. Searches across `skills/` found no equivalent rule. (`CLAUDE.md:140`; `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:101`, `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:200`; `skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md:10`.) **PASS.**

9. The decision is supported as a conservative treatment of reviewer-readable instruction text. The design records that rationale, the skill documents instruction text steering reviewers, and the tests explicitly explain that ordinary file reading can make an instruction surface relevant independently of automatic discovery. This supports retaining coverage without assuming every directory is automatically discovered. ([design:56](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md:56); `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:61`, `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:131`; `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:282`.) **PASS.**

10. Task 3 supplies the region, a single assertion containing its entire body, and the declaration. That matches the repository’s mechanics. Physical wrapping and indentation matter to this assertion; `read_text()` normalizes newline conventions, so this is not a byte-level CRLF/LF pin. (`evals/multi-model-verify/test_contract_coverage.py:620`, `evals/multi-model-verify/test_contract_coverage.py:770`; `evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py:38`, `evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py:842`; [plan:415](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:415), [plan:441](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:441), [plan:467](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:467).) **PASS.**
