12. These tests establish two examples, not the claimed universal property. The corrupted fixture contains invalid UTF-8, so it exercises the reader’s catch rather than hostile records reaching the parser and renderer. Neither test exercises construction collisions, aliases, write failure, or a corrupted sidecar with an unchanged source. The helper also builds with `-SkipProbe`, leaving override collisions outside these tests. ([plan:232](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:232), [plan:246](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:246), [plan:320](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:320); `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:119`, `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:1532`.) **FIX: add valid hostile-manifest cases, construction collision/alias cases, write-failure cases, and unchanged-source controls.**

**S1. Class sweep**

The following instances are evidenced beyond the numbered verdicts:

- **The diagnostic makes an unsupported causal inference.** A sidecar replaced with the live manifest produces no reported content differences even when content drift caused the refusal. The message then asserts that the status listing itself changed. A mutable advisory comparison cannot establish that conclusion. Malformed records are also silently dropped, and duplicate keys overwrite earlier records. Replace the conclusion with “the advisory manifest did not identify the cause,” and report malformed input as unavailable. ([plan:274](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:274), [plan:281](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:281), [plan:353](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:353).)

- **Manifest membership does not establish file appearance or disappearance.** Editing a previously clean tracked file adds it to this manifest; restoring a dirty tracked file to HEAD removes it. Both files can exist throughout. The proposed `appeared` and `vanished` labels therefore overstate what the comparison measures. Label these as entering or leaving manifest coverage, or obtain separate evidence of existence. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:437`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:473`; [plan:292](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:292).)

- **Case-only pathname changes disappear from the diagnostic.** The proposed PowerShell hashtables compare string keys without case sensitivity. In-memory execution on both hosts returned empty drift groups for `File.txt` versus `file.txt` with identical hashes, although the digest incorporates the original strings. Use ordinal key comparison when explaining the digest’s inputs. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:655`; [plan:277](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:277), [plan:283](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:283).)

node.exe: C:\Users\Brandon\AppData\Local\npm-global\codex.ps1:22
Line |
  22 |  …    $input | & "node$exe"  "$basedir/node_modules/@openai/codex/bin/co …
     |                ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
     | Cannot process the XML from the 'Error' stream of 'C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe': Data at the root level is
     | invalid. Line 1, position 1.

- **“Returns nothing” misstates PowerShell output semantics.** `Write-Output` emits values a caller can capture and branch on. The relevant protection is that this caller ignores those values and exits unconditionally. Also, the function’s catch encloses only reading; it does not establish the advertised “never throws” guarantee for parsing and output. ([plan:316](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:316), [plan:320](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:320), [plan:333](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:333), [plan:349](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:349).)

- **The quiet-period prose overstates enforcement and quota consumption.** The mechanism samples state, misses reverted mutations, and expressly excludes raw-byte changes that Git still reports clean. Preparation and pre-client refusals occur before the client invocation, so those failures do not inherently spend a reviewer round. Keep the conservative operational rule while stating these measurement limits. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:727`; `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:286`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:291`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:305`; [plan:416](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:416).)

- **“Never writes to the real tree” contradicts the tool’s own account of Git status.** The header makes the absolute promise, while the directory-link guard explains that status captures write the source repository’s index. Qualify the promise or suppress those optional writes. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:19`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1101`.)

- **Construction comments claim more than endpoint comparisons prove.** The comment says the retained value distinguishes a source that moved and moved back. Equality at the observed endpoints cannot do so. The existing identity contract already acknowledges that intermediate bytes can reach the copy despite matching endpoints. Align the comments with that contract. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1308`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1382`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1416`; `skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md:723`.)

- **Verification’s same-directory protection checks spelling rather than filesystem identity.** It compares provider-resolved strings, then proceeds without the build’s reparse-point checks. A junction alias of a source directory can have a different spelling while reaching the same files. The claimed same-directory refusal therefore exceeds this comparison. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:734`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:759`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:787`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1258`.)

- **Extra-input coverage can fail silently.** The tool promises to copy and record each extra input, but `Copy-Item` has neither terminating error handling nor a success check. A nonterminating copy failure can leave an input absent; the subsequent manifest cannot discover an absent file that never entered status. Make failed copies explicit refusals. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:39`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:519`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1554`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1684`.)

- **Generated wrappers do not preserve arbitrary accepted path text.** Paths are embedded in the wrapper, which is then written as ASCII. An accepted path containing `é` becomes `?`; the in-memory encoding probe confirmed this. Use an encoding readable without loss on both hosts. Separately, the printed command places paths in expandable double-quoted strings without escaping `$` or backticks; a legal path component such as `$(1+1)` becomes executable expression syntax. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:341`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:570`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:591`.)

- **Classification redemption is not exclusive.** Reading and comparing `classifying:<nonce>` does not reserve ownership atomically. Two callers can both pass that comparison before either writes the final state. The “this call owns the reservation” assertion needs exclusive access or a narrower guarantee. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:50`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:663`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:675`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:682`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:644`.)

- **Dispatch failure guarantees exceed the error paths.** A receipt write failure after creation can leave a partial receipt, contradicting “no receipt.” Wrapper verification failures throw before classification, so their exit cannot carry the promised classification state. An oversized integer in `exit` passes the regex and then throws during the unchecked integer conversion instead of following the stated classification mapping. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:121`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:572`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:580`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:287`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:311`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:790`.)

**S2. Other forms**

- **Destructive or disruptive writes:** besides the alias case in claims 5–6, an explicitly supplied `-OverrideOut <MirrorPath>.source-manifest` passes the existing separation checks. The probe writes its verified override there; the proposed sidecar writer then overwrites it. The documented wrapper consequently refuses its hash. Skip advisory creation on collisions without touching the existing artifact. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:960`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1725`; `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:184`; [plan:114](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:114), [plan:124](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:124).)

- **Misleading the operator:** forged valid records can blame harmless files or suppress the real content change. The output contains no advisory warning beside the claims, and its fallback positively attributes the cause to status metadata. The reference’s “Build again” advice can therefore encourage replacing evidence before investigating the actual change. Put the uncertainty in the emitted explanation itself. ([plan:281](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:281), [plan:342](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:342), [plan:354](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:354), [plan:537](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:537).)

- **Resource exhaustion and output injection:** `ReadAllLines` reads an unbounded file, the parser stores and sorts unbounded records, and the twenty-name limit leaves each printed name unbounded. Sidecar names bypass `Test-SupportedPathname`, allowing control characters and terminal escapes into output. Bound input bytes, record lengths, and total output; escape control characters. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:254`; [plan:279](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:279), [plan:302](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:302), [plan:322](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:322), [plan:345](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:345).)

- **Root-path construction:** the proposed helper maps `D:\` to `D:.source-manifest`, a drive-relative path rather than a sibling. The in-memory probe confirmed that result. Reject root-shaped inputs for advisory placement or derive a separately validated destination. ([plan:102](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:102).)

- **Encoding corruption:** a first pathname beginning with U+FEFF is accepted by the pathname guard. The BOM-free writer emits those initial bytes as pathname content, but the BOM-detecting reader consumes them as an encoding marker. The in-memory stream-reader probe reproduced that character loss. Use explicit byte decoding that preserves pathname data. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:249`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:254`; [plan:113](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:113), [plan:322](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:322).)

- **Diagnostics remain hidden during wrapper failures:** both wrapper verifications redirect their details into `mirror.verify`, then throw a generic message. The proposed reference only describes “lines beneath” the refusal and gives no route to that file. Document the log location or surface a bounded explanation through the wrapper. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:286`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:310`; [plan:542](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:542).)
